# Paper B (route R1) — canonical plan

**Status:** canonical from 9 October 2026; revised 12 October 2026 (R1-REFRAME, R1-INDEX and R1-GATE-FIRST in §1). This is the only plan for Paper B. Every plan, runbook, decision sheet, prompt pack and review written for the earlier Paper B route is archived under `archive/old_b_2026-10-09/` and has no authority.

**Owner:** Dowling Wong (author; decides). This plan was drafted by an AI agent (Claude, coordinating session) from the author's decisions of 9 October 2026 and two AI-assisted inputs in `docs/r1/inputs/`. None of this is human or external review.

## 2. The question

In a governed autoresearch campaign, an LLM manager proposes bounded edits to a training script and a worker trains. A control plane keeps an edit only when the parsed metric strictly beats the incumbent. This default rule makes measurable errors:

1. **Wasted runs.** Invalid trials consume budget. Healthy autoresearch seeds showed invalid rates of 0.10–0.30.
2. **Noise-driven keeps and discards.** As an illustration only, from the old ResNet-trigger node (removed from R1): the measured seed s.d. is about 0.0138 AUC. Assuming a cross-config correlation of 0.5, a true ±0.005 change is decided wrongly about 36 % of the time. Verifying with 1 or 3 extra seeds lowers this only to about 30 % or 23 %.

The correlation is assumed; Phase 0 measures it (inputs §4.4).

**Question (revised 12 Oct, R1-REFRAME).** The governed loop is a noisy sequential selection problem with an incumbent. Each trial draws a candidate, observes one noisy metric, and accepts or rejects it against an incumbent whose own value is a noisy, selected draw. Three sub-questions:

1. **When is verification worth its cost?** Derive the value of verify (r paired fresh-seed runs) and of a raised keep threshold as functions of the observed margin |Δ̂|/σ_n, the cross-config correlation ϱ and the remaining budget.
2. **How biased is the incumbent?** Characterise the winner's-curse bias of `current_best` (a maximum of noisy draws) and how it lowers the acceptance rate of later true improvements, i.e. how it drives stagnation.
3. **How much does learning add?** Above the index policy that follows from 1–2 (KG-style or PACE-style), how much does a learned configurator gain at matched compute, and where does LLM self-control sit?

The 9 Oct question (LLM vs configurator vs hybrid; does the problem need RL) survives inside 3. It is no longer the headline.

## 3. Planned contributions (claims are falsifiable; none is established)

> **18 Oct 2026 (R1-C3, R1-C4, §10): current contributions.** The table below the box is kept as history.
> - **C1** — measured control errors (unchanged), including wasted runs on duplicate and inert edits.
> - **C2** — theory of acceptance under noise: T2 plus `theory/T4_FEEDBACK_PROPOSER.md`. T4 covers the ledger-dependent proposer, the per-proposal gain bound and skip rule, the winner's curse for any adaptive proposer, and decision independence. Its empirical checks (Proposition 7 bound vs realised gain, Proposition 8 screening term, the T4 §7 SFT-vs-GRPO prediction) use C3's labels and are reported with C3.
> - **C3** — learned per-proposal prior under the C2 rule: the A0–A6 ladder and the C3 gate (`docs/r1/C3_DESIGN.md`).
> - **C4 (secondary)** — **proposer feedback**: (i) a test of the decision-independence condition (T4 Proposition 10) on forced-decision branch pairs at matched histories; (ii) a debiased-memory intervention that shows the manager the run-free posterior estimate of the incumbent instead of the raw record (T4 §5). C4 also decides whether online RL (A7) is warranted. A small effect is reported as such; C4 then shrinks without affecting C1–C3.

| ID | Contribution | Kind | Evidence that would establish it |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Measured control errors in governed autoresearch:** false keep, false discard and wasted-run rates per node, from noise-floor measurements (training seed and split seed separately) and fresh-seed audits of ledgered candidates. | Phenomenon | Phase 0–1 audits on nodes with real training- or split-seed noise (D21) |
| C2 | **Theory of incremental acceptance under noise** (R1-REFRAME). The governed loop as noisy sequential selection with an incumbent: (i) the value of verify and of a raised keep threshold as functions of \|Δ̂\|/σ_n, ϱ and remaining budget; (ii) the winner's-curse bias of `current_best` and its effect on later acceptance (stagnation); (iii) the index policy that follows (KG-style or PACE-style, D23). Built on ranking & selection, knowledge gradient, racing and best-arm identification. The identification ladder Q^ρ → V^L0 → V^π and the rule for when RL is needed remain as a secondary part. | Formulation and theory (main) | Full proofs; predictions checked against measured σ_n and ϱ, and against simulation |
| C3 | **How much does learning add above the index policy?** (R1-INDEX). On held-out instances at matched compute, the trained configurator (and hybrid H1) is compared with three primary comparators: the C2 index policy, a PACE-style gate and a noise-aware threshold. LLM self-control is an arm, with a stronger manager at least as a deployment check (D22). An action ablation shows where any gain comes from. | Main empirical result | Phase 3 deployment |
| C4 | **Configurator training recipe** (secondary). Full-information supervised learning on branch labels, then RL only if the ladder shows it is needed. Built only after the headroom gate passes (R1-GATE-FIRST). | Method, secondary | Phase 2–3 |

**Fallback if the headroom gate fails (RUNBOOK 0.4b pre-check or 1.5 gate).** If the hindsight oracle cannot beat the best simple baseline by the practical target, nothing is learned and no configurator is built. The paper becomes **C1 + C2 theory**, plus the measured statement that on these nodes there is no room for learning above the index policy. That is a complete TMLR paper.

**Novelty position** (inputs/NOVELTY_SWEEP): no prior work trains a policy for when and how to intervene in an autoresearch keep/discard loop. Each piece, however, has close neighbours:
- Harness-MDP (2607.05458): small offline-RL controller over a frozen LLM;
- Centaur (2603.24647): LLM plus CMA-ES on autoresearch, untrained;
- PACE (2606.08106): statistical commit gate;
- COTA (2608.21027): tiny advisor trained on counterfactual branches;
- PrimeScientist (2609.17846): budgeted allocation over autoresearch;
- Rehearse (2607.27687): the LLM judge degrades late in a campaign.

The paper must win on the **combination** and on three differentiators (revised 12 Oct):
1. a measured, unplanted noise mechanism (C1);
2. **a theory of acceptance under noise that is B's own** (C2). It connects the agent loop to ranking & selection, knowledge gradient, racing and best-arm identification. What those classical results do not cover: a proposer whose proposal distribution depends on the ledger, an incumbent-relative keep with restore, and a winner's-curse bias that feeds back into the proposer;
3. a sharp learning question: the gain above the theory-grounded index policy at matched compute, with an explicit LLM self-control arm (C3).

Same-state counterfactual labels with stated identification conditions stay as a supporting point, not a differentiator. Restore is bit-exact on the CPU nodes (STATUS, L7), so p ≈ 0 there, and p-aware labels are unlikely to change a decision.

Three risks have to be neutralised in the experiments themselves:
- "it is just HPO scheduling": HPO baselines plus an action ablation;
- "a small controller over an LLM is known": a self-control failure, a hybrid result, and transfer;
- "branch labels are standard": measured p, plus a case where p-aware labels change a decision;
- "it is just ranking & selection / racing" (added 12 Oct): state what the classical models assume (i.i.d. arms, a fixed arm set, no incumbent restore), show where the loop departs, and include racing and KG-style baselines.

The sweep is rerun before submission. From 12 Oct it also covers ranking & selection, knowledge gradient (Frazier & Powell), racing (F-Race / irace) and best-arm identification, which the 9 Oct sweep did not cover.

## 4. Setting and estimands (summary; full text in inputs §1–§5)

**Instance and episode**
- An instance is ι = (node, seed, manager, compute budget C, start config).
- An episode is one campaign.
- Decision epochs inside a trial:
  - E0, before the proposal;
  - E1, after the proposal and before execution;
  - E2, after the metric and before commit.

**Actions**
- Core set, proposed in D3: continue, skip (E1), revise (E1), verify with a frozen response rule that can demote a noise keep (E2).
- stop is added if the budget unit is compute (D1).
- Extensions: scope/step-size, memory-mode switch, low fidelity / early stop, accept-worse.

**Outcome.** Y is the fresh-seed audited improvement of the final incumbent over the start config, normalised per node. The audit uses common seeds across arms and lies outside the campaign budget. The in-run `current_best` is never the outcome.

**Estimands**
- **Primary: V^π.** The deployed policy's value at matched compute and LLM-call cap, on held-out instances.
- Secondary: one-step Q^ρ from branch labels, and V^L0.
- No Markov claim for any compressed state.

**Where Paper A enters**
- Branch labels come from restored states, so Paper A's discrepancy allowance p qualifies them: 2p sufficient, p/(1−p) necessary, Γ in between.
- Deployment contrasts involve no restoration, so **p does not tax the primary result**. The old N1 problem disappears.
- Seed and GPU nondeterminism count as kernel noise, not discrepancy (D5, proposed).

## 5. Arms and baselines

**Primary comparator (R1-INDEX, 12 Oct).** Every learned arm must beat the C2 index policy (KG-style or PACE-style, D23). The primary contrasts are configurator − index policy and H1 − index policy. Configurator vs LLM self-control is secondary.

**Arms**
1. **LLM self-control.** A side query on a forked context returns an action and per-action scores. It never enters the manager context, and its tokens are charged to the arm. Branch data use the pinned local manager (D15). A stronger manager (D22) runs at least as a deployment check, so the arm is not a strawman. *(Superseded 3 Nov by R1-MAIN-PROPOSER: branch data and the self-control arm use the Track X pick, thinking off; the 7B is the second source.)*
2. **Trained configurator.** Architecture is open; see §7.
3. **Hybrid H1.** The configurator decides *whether/when*; the LLM decides *how*.
4. **Hybrid H2** (secondary; dropped first if the matrix must shrink). The LLM's scores and rationale are inputs to the configurator.

**Baselines** (trimmed 12 Oct)

> **4 Nov (R1-BX-D7, R1-BX-D9, R1-BX-D13):** the main table adds two practitioner baselines, the Karpathy default (always execute, single run, keep if Δ̂ > 0) and fixed-r replication + t-test; the self-control arm is A4 (the Track X pick, zero-shot, isotonic-calibrated), and R-B is an optional trained-self-control ablation; TabPFN gets an information-matched comparison.

Required:
- ρ, the default rule;
- the C2 index policy (KG-style or PACE-style);
- a PACE-style anytime-valid commit gate;
- a noise-aware keep threshold;
- a racing-style re-evaluation rule (F-Race / irace style);
- harness heuristics: repeated-bad and doom-loop skip;
- random at matched realised budget;
- an offline-RL (AWR) MLP controller in the style of Harness-MDP;
- a hindsight oracle on the branch table, used for headroom only.

Secondary (appendix; dropped first if compute is short):
- fixed schedules;
- a risk-ranked vs advantage-ranked comparator on Z;
- the two-step continuation-value rule (Longstaff–Schwartz / FQE) for budgeted verify;
- successive halving / ASHA, for the fidelity extension only.

**Matching.**
- Every arm faces the same instances, manager, compute C, LLM-call cap and intervention cap.
- All arms share one online spending rule and calibrate it with the same tuning budget on development instances.
- Information parity is checked mechanically by `matching_report`, carried over (§8).

**Inference.** Cluster bootstrap by instance, results by node, and leave-one-node-out as a descriptive check.

## 6. Data and instances (agent benchmarks; D20 proposal)

> **Superseded 4 Nov (R1-BX-D1, R1-BX-D2, R1-BX-D4, R1-BX-D5).** Tiers: **T0** = the 8 CPU nodes (controlled testbed, single-parameter low-interaction condition); **T1** = label + evaluation GPU tasks from autoresearch, MLGym and MLAgentBench (1GC-7RC replaced 4 Nov 12:45, R1-1GC-FALLBACK) (fixed step budgets, code-level diffs); **T2** = evaluation only. Target 12–16 real tasks from ≥ 3 suites and ≥ 5 domains; the suites' task assets become harness nodes, their agent frameworks are not used. Primary generalisation is leave-one-suite-out. MLE-bench and RE-Bench are not used. The table and generalisation design below are kept as history.

The decision is made (R1-BENCH): no ResNet; agent benchmarks throughout. Which benchmarks is still open. Every environment must have the R1 loop shape: propose an edit, run, evaluate, keep or discard. A benchmark without a noisy keep decision cannot show C1.

| Pool | Candidates | Why | Cost and status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Training workhorse (many cheap instances) | OpenML / AutoML-style tabular tasks; existing harness nodes `lr_synthetic`, `openml_*` | Hundreds of instances, CPU-cheap, real seed noise | Seconds per run. 10–20+ tasks to add (small new code). |
| Noisy CPU nodes (added 12 Oct, D21) | SGD-trained small MLP / small CNN nodes on CPU where the training seed moves the metric; existing nodes run with the split seed varied | C1 and C2 need nodes where the noise exists. On `lr_synthetic` the training seed moves val_score by ≤ 1e-4 while the split seed moves it by ~0.01 (STATUS, 9 Oct). The five OpenML nodes are deterministic at a fixed seed. | Small new code (L8b); E1 measures them |
| Agent benchmarks, primary | **MLGym-Bench** (13 open-ended AI-research tasks; Gym interface; open-sourced) [arXiv 2502.14499]; **MLAgentBench** (an adapter already exists in the harness) | Recognised ML-research-agent benchmarks; MLGym is built for RL training of agents | Per-task compute varies; the 0.4 pilot measures it. Light tasks for training, heavy ones held out. |
| Agent benchmark, held out | **MLE-bench Lite** (low-complexity Kaggle split) [arXiv 2410.07095] | Cross-benchmark generalisation is the strongest test of the configurator | Kaggle data and terms, larger compute; evaluation only. |
| Deployment showcase | autoresearch (Karpathy-style, GPU) | The setting the story starts from | About 5 min per run; deployment only, never branched. |
| Possible instance generator | ML-AutoResearch auto-generated environments [arXiv 2603.17216] | Many synthetic ML-research environments | Release not confirmed. It trains the agent by SFT, not a controller, so it is a neighbour to cite. |
| Excluded | ResNet-trigger; RE-Bench (8-hour H100 tasks); general agent benchmarks (τ-bench, WebShop, SWE-bench) unless D20 says otherwise | No ResNet by decision; RE-Bench is too costly; general benchmarks lack the keep/discard loop and sit on Harness-MDP's and COTA's turf | — |

**Generalisation design (proposed):** train on the OpenML pool and MLGym light tasks; hold out MLAgentBench and the MLE-bench Lite subset; report leave-one-benchmark-out.

- **The earlier trials.** The 1,355 trials behind the ETAAI workshop paper are lost, and only aggregate tables survive. They are in the workshop-paper repository (`~/Documents/A-Governed-Harness-for-Auditable-LLM-Driven-ML-Experimentation/tables/*.csv`, also a submodule of the harness), for example `governance_metrics.csv`. These are context only. **All R1 numbers come from new runs**, starting with the Phase 0 pilot.
- **Workshop numbers cannot be re-audited.** The `ClawWorker` sign issue (RUNBOOK 0.2, item 2) cannot be checked against the lost ledgers. R1 never reuses workshop-paper numbers as evidence.
- **Harness code.** Local clone at `~/Documents/autoresearch_harness`, cloned 9 Oct 2026 at `da8dcbd`, with submodules not initialised. R1 work goes on branch `r1` (D14).

## 7. Configurator architecture (proposal for discussion; D16 open)

> **Historical proposal; superseded in part.** See §10 R1-C3, R1-C3-BASE, R1-C3-CONTROL and R1-C3-POST for the adopted ladder, base pool and separated belief/action outputs. The later rows drop CLM and the greedy-bin action design; this section is retained for provenance.

> **Deferred (R1-GATE-FIRST, 12 Oct).** No architecture is selected and no configurator scaffold is built until the headroom gate passes (RUNBOOK 0.4b pre-check, then 1.5). This section is context for that later decision, not a work item. If the gate fails, it is not needed.

**Verified open candidates** (repository pages read 9 Oct 2026):

| Candidate | What it is | Post-training | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Kev** (`github.com/jaredpalmer/kev`) | Small Jev-like typed decision models: `noul` (probability), `choice` (option probabilities and confidence), `score` (ordinal). Calibrated by default. Sizes 0.8B / 4B / 9B (Qwen3.5-Base) and 27B. API-compatible with the TypeSafe SDK. Apache-2.0. | LoRA (rank 16) cross-entropy fine-tuning on own labels. RL is not in the repo; policy gradient can be applied directly to the `choice` distribution. | **Recommended primary.** It natively emits the calibrated per-action probabilities the shared spending rule needs. |
| **CLM** (`github.com/Contrastive-LM/CLM`) | Contrastive state/action dual encoder on frozen Qwen3-8B with about 20M-parameter projection heads; typed Noul/Choice/Score plus `rank`. Post-trained on about 1M agent trajectories. Apache-2.0. Served through a vLLM pooling server. | Fine-tune the heads (`train/finetune.py`); RL on the heads is possible. | Ablation: does a frozen representation with a light head suffice? |
| Generative small LLM (Qwen3 / Qwen3.5, 0.6–4B) via TRL, verl or MLX-LM-LoRA | Writes reasoning, then an action | SFT, then GRPO | Ablation: does free-text reasoning add anything over typed decisions? It can also produce the *how* (revision text). |
| Tabular: LightGBM / ridge / **TabPFN-2** (weights under the Prior Labs License: Apache-2.0 plus attribution; versions 2.5 and later are non-commercial); offline RL via d3rlpy (IQL / CQL / AWR-style MLP) | Structured ledger features | Supervised; offline RL | **Required strong baselines.** The AWR-MLP mirrors Harness-MDP. |

**Proposed design (to discuss).**

- **Primary configurator: Kev at two sizes, 0.8B and 4B.** Each epoch is posed as typed questions, for example:
  - E1: `choice{execute, skip, revise}`;
  - E2: `noul`: "is this keep explained by noise?" and `choice{commit, verify}`.
- **Post-training ladder** (D8):
  1. LoRA cross-entropy on full-information branch labels (the best action, with calibration);
  2. policy-gradient / GRPO on the `choice` distribution with rewards from the counterfactual table;
  3. online RL on CPU instances, only if the ladder shows greedy deployment underperforms.
- **Comparisons at matched information:** Kev vs CLM heads vs generative LLM vs tabular / TabPFN vs AWR-MLP vs LLM self-control. Plus a size ladder of 0.8B → 4B (→ 9B if needed).
- **Natural hybrid H1:** Kev decides *whether / when*; the manager LLM decides *how*.

**Why this is interesting.** It asks a question the closest prior work does not: does a calibrated typed-decision model, post-trained on counterfactual labels, beat both generative reasoning policies and tabular controllers at controlling an agent loop?

**Data-scale caution.** LoRA fine-tuning needs hundreds to thousands of labelled decisions. That is why the cheap OpenML pool carries training (§6).

**Interface requirements** (inputs §8):
- input (Z, M, epoch);
- output a masked action distribution plus one scalar score per action;
- ≤ ~1 s latency per call;
- deterministic and logged as a `control_decision` event;
- never sees audit or test metrics, sibling outcomes or future records;
- never writes the ledger.

## 8. Old Paper B evaluated for R1

Criterion: carry an item only if it is useful for R1, cheap to adapt, and has low risk of importing old assumptions.

**Carry over: code, adapted in place (`experiment_scaffold/src/causal_branch/control/`)**

| Module | Use in R1 | Adaptation |
|---|---|---|
| `schema.py`, `branch_tree.py` | Branch records, leak-safe learner projection, integrity status distinct from outcome | Sibling sets per trial epoch instead of three fixed leaves |
| `evaluate.py`, `evaluate_tree.py`, `bounds.py` | Cluster bootstrap with shared draws; Hoeffding radius | Cluster = instance |
| `controllers.py` (`matching_report`) | Information / capacity / tuning parity across arms | New arm list |
| `learners.py` | Ridge, utility-deficit and failure-risk learners | Tabular baselines; the risk-vs-advantage comparator |
| `timing.py` | Two-step continuation-value rule | Baseline for budgeted verify |
| `configurator/` | Serialiser rules, encoder slot, heads | Architecture family (c); serialiser retargeted to ledger events |
| `allocator.py` | Two-phase batch allocator | Offline batch evaluation only; deployment uses the online spending rule |
| `replay_validity.py` | Population allowance | Appendix only |

**Carry over: principles**
- original-population estimands: no conditioning on valid metrics; skipped and invalid trials stay in;
- collection failure is an integrity status, never a utility value;
- treatments are executed by the control plane and are never optional for the LLM (the old D-COMPLY);
- primary contrasts are declared before the data that tests them;
- a hindsight-oracle headroom gate runs before training;
- evidence runs need an author-made authorization note;
- AI-assisted work is labelled as such.

**Archived (no authority; kept for provenance)**
- every file of the old `docs/paper_split/`: plans, runbooks, decision sheets, reviews, prompts, claims ledger, scope;
- the candidate-consistency task and its realism reports;
- the N1 / N2 hypotheses, the 0.02 U target, P-B1, and the cutoffs;
- the Pi-backed executor plans, the Devstral / Ada binding plan, and the DAC configurator amendment as a framing document.

**Legacy code and manuscript** (moved to `archive/old_b_2026-10-09/code/` on 18 Oct 2026; see §10)
- `experiments/paper_b/task_cc*`, the Pi path in `control/executor.py`, `control/world.py`, `control/tree.py`;
- `protocols/paper_b/` (contracts v1–v3, launch validator);
- `papers/paper_b/` (the old manuscript);
- release scripts.

Historical wording: they were retained for tests and release tooling and RUNBOOK task 0.1 was to decide the move. The move was subsequently completed under the 18 Oct RUNBOOK 0.1 row in §10; the archive remains read-only history.

## 9. Venue, quality bar and timeline

**Venue (decided 9 Oct): TMLR.**

> **Superseded 24 Oct (R1-VENUE-3): ICML 2027.** Only the venue changed; the timeline and quality bar below stay as they are.
- TMLR accepts when claims are supported and of interest to part of its audience. There is no novelty-magnitude bar, and there is no deadline pressure.
- Paper A also targets TMLR. Cite it anonymously as a companion.
- TMLR pauses over the holidays; check the dates before planning a submission around them.
- A conference version stays possible if the results are ready before a suitable deadline. Decide at the end of Phase 3; nothing is planned around one.

**Quality bar: ICML main-track standard (decided 9 Oct).** Concretely, the paper must meet all of the following before submission.

1. **Clarity and length.** The core argument is concise, and the rest goes to the appendix. No template or page count is fixed yet (R1-FORMAT). TMLR's own regular/long-submission threshold is checked when the format is chosen.
2. **Baselines.**
   - The strongest relevant baselines, re-implemented and tuned with the same budget as our arms.
   - At least: the C2 index policy, a PACE-style gate, a racing-style rule, an AWR-MLP controller in the style of Harness-MDP, a noise-aware threshold, harness heuristics, and HPO-style schedulers where the action is HPO-like.
   - No strawmen. The tuning protocol is identical for every arm, and `matching_report` checks it.
3. **Statistics.**
   - Primary contrasts, sample sizes and analysis are pre-declared.
   - Cluster-bootstrap intervals by instance, with multiplicity stated.
   - Held-out instances and a held-out node.
   - Effect sizes stated in practical units.
4. **Ablations and mechanism.**
   - An action ablation.
   - Information ablations (with and without LLM text, with and without remaining budget).
   - A model-size ablation for family (b), only if family (b) is trained.
   - The ladder V^ρ ≤ V^L0 ≤ V^greedy ≤ V^RL.
5. **Generalisation.** Transfer to a held-out benchmark (leave-one-benchmark-out) and a second manager model, both reported. *(4 Nov, R1-BX-D1/D6: the primary test is leave-one-suite-out over three suites; a second proposer, an API model, enters closed-loop evaluation.)* *(3 Nov, R1-MAIN-PROPOSER: the second manager is `qwen2.5-coder:7b`, sweep 1.)*
6. **Theory.**
   - C2 (acceptance under noise) is the main theory. Its statements carry full proofs, an AI-assisted audit and the author's own check, and its predictions are checked against measured σ_n and ϱ.
   - Assumptions are stated where they are used.
7. **Reproducibility.**
   - Code, configs, seeds and compute reported per arm.
   - An anonymised artifact.
   - Every number regenerated by a script from committed data.
8. **Positioning.**
   - The novelty sweep is rerun within 4 weeks of submission.
   - An explicit differentiator against each of the closest works (§3), including ranking & selection, knowledge gradient, racing and best-arm identification.
9. **Honesty.**
   - Negative and null results reported.
   - AI assistance disclosed.
   - Workshop-paper overlap disclosed.

| D24 | **Manager memory mode.** Primary proposal law for every LLM-backed R1 run: `append_only_summary`. The collector takes the memory mode from the manifest, never as a literal; manifests without the field mean `none` (historical runs). Branch continuations use the same memory mode as their root. |
| D25 | **Node-declared active keys.** Each node may declare which editable keys are active for the current configuration (for the OpenML template: keyed by `model_type`, plus dataset-level exclusions such as `imputer` without categorical columns). The manager is shown only active keys; a proposal naming an inactive key is rejected before any worker run, as a validation failure the manager may revise (it costs LLM calls, not runs). The scripted stand-in reads the same declaration instead of its private `OPENML_ACTIVE`, and its proposal sequence for existing seeds must stay identical. Nodes without a declaration behave exactly as before. All other nodes are audited for inert keys before E3. |

| D27 | **No-op and duplicate edits.** Record-only flags on every trial: effective config equal to the incumbent's, equal to an earlier evaluated config, or metric bit-identical to the incumbent's. They count as wasted runs in C1 and change no behaviour in the primary arms. Add an E1 harness-heuristic arm that skips a known no-op or duplicate (the PLAN §5 doom-loop skip). |
| D28 | **Node liveness check before LLM-backed collection.** Per node, with the chosen memory mode: 1–2 instances, W = 10, no branching. Pass requires at least one keep after trial 1, at least 50 % of trials with a metric different from the incumbent's, and an invalid rate below 30 % (thresholds as proposed, adopted). A failing node is fixed or excluded and recorded in STATUS; the batch runner enforces the check as a STOP. |

| Duplicate ≠ no-op (19 Oct) | **Rule-detectable proposals are split into two kinds.** A *no-op* leaves the incumbent's active configuration unchanged (Δ = 0). A *duplicate* repeats an earlier evaluated non-incumbent configuration: its result is known, Δ is generally ≠ 0, and under ρ it can never be kept (T4 Corollary 8a(iii)). The rule layer (A2+) skips both. Only no-ops get the zero label; duplicates get no training label, and in offline evaluation an arm without the rule layer that keeps one is charged its audited Δ (or the logged Δ̂ as a counted proxy). Implemented in `r1/c3/` (`noop`, `duplicate`, `rule_skip`, `known_metric`). Records the author's "我同意拆开，去改". |
| R1-COMPUTE (Paper A coordination, 19 Oct) | **No Paper B GPU workload starts on Ada while a Paper A live campaign there is waiting for a card.** Paper A's replay panel v4 live (repository `agent-checkpoint-intervention`, branch `paper-a-v4-ada`, RUN `results/paper_a/replay_panel_v4/live_20261018T234416Z`) needs a fully idle card for its first start. Paper B's co-tenancy rule (13 Oct) would otherwise let a Paper B process occupy a card that was about to become idle. Before any Paper B GPU launch on Ada (the C3 label sweep, a memory-on branch run, or anything that starts Ollama or training), the operator: (i) checks the Paper A run's status; (ii) proceeds only if no Paper A live campaign is waiting for a card, i.e. it has its card and is computing, or it has finished or stopped. Otherwise the operator waits or runs the workload on Deepthought instead. CPU-only and file-only Paper B work (diagnostics, audits on CPU, analysis) is unaffected. Paper B never stops, signals or competes with Paper A processes. Records the author's "把这条协调规则写进 PLAN". |

| ID | Decision (decided 22 Oct 2026 by the author) |
|---|---|
| Duplicates (22 Oct; confirms 19 Oct) | **Option A.** Confirms the 19 Oct "Duplicate ≠ no-op" row: three rule-detectable kinds — *inactive edit* (changes only keys inactive for the node's model; Δ = 0), *incumbent repeat* (returns to the incumbent's active configuration; Δ = 0), both counted as **no-ops**; and *duplicate* (repeats an earlier evaluated non-incumbent configuration; result known from the ledger, Δ generally ≠ 0). The rule layer (A2+) skips all three, and the run each skip saves is credited to A2 as in the run-accounting row below. Duplicates are **excluded from LM/tabular training and from the learned-arm evaluation set**; they are counted separately. **New:** C1 reports the duplicate rate and the runs they would cost as wasted runs, per node and memory mode, next to the published 28–63 % ("Visible but Not Avoided", arXiv 2610.15738). |
| g (22 Oct) | **Option A.** Run price g = 0.1 σ_node, where σ_node is the node's paired comparison-noise sd of Δ̂ (E1, the same σ that standardises the bins). Sensitivity values g ∈ {0.05, 0.2} σ_node are declared now and reported, but **the C3 gate is judged at g = 0.1 σ_node only.** g is fixed before any gate is computed on sweep data and is not changed afterwards. Replaces C3_DESIGN §1 "g is tuned on development instances". |
| Run accounting in the 20-run scaling (22 Oct) | **Option A.** In the primary analysis a skip is credited at the price g (reward r = Δ_audit · keep − g · execute); freed runs are **not** re-spent. This is a stated convention, consistent with g being declared as the value of one run, not a derived opportunity cost; the report says so. Sensitivity (wording corrected 22 Oct at acceptance; the author's choice of option A is unchanged): a literal replay is impossible because root ledgers hold exactly one proposal per run, so no logged proposal exists beyond the end of a campaign. Instead, freed runs are re-spent on further proposals exchangeable with the arm's own, under a cap of c × W LLM proposals per campaign: value = m × min(W, e·c·W), with m the arm's mean realised reward per executed proposal and e its execution rate. c = 1 equals the primary; c = 2 is reported as the sensitivity and c = ∞ as an optimistic upper bound. No pass/fail is attached to either. |
| Node pool (22 Oct) | Extends D21. Candidates to reach ≥ 8 qualifying nodes, in order: `openml_credit_approval` and `openml_diabetes` (qualify only if the 30-seed `"vary"` re-measurement shows non-trivial noise under D21's criterion); `openml_credit_g` (fetch its data cache, then measure). If fewer than 8 qualify, add 2–3 further OpenML classification datasets, measured the same way. The re-measurement is CPU-only (Mac or Deepthought). |
| `digits_mlp` (22 Oct) | **Repair rather than exclude.** It is the only node with a positive gate quantity in the pilot. Keep it in the pool; diagnose the Ollama SIGTERM and proposer repetition, then re-run D28 liveness. It stays out of memory-on collection until D28 passes. If it cannot pass, the author decides between keeping it memory-off only and exclusion, with the diagnosis in hand. |

**Decided 22 Oct 2026 by the author (noise-node rule and pool extension, after the 30-seed re-measurement).** These rows are fixed, not proposals. The author agreed to all four recommendations of the coordinating session ("我同意，把这四条写进 PLAN §10"). Declared **before** any 30-seed measurement of the original five nodes or of the new candidates.

| ID | Decision (decided 22 Oct 2026 by the author) |
|---|---|
| D21 rule (22 Oct) | **Adopted, amending E1's proposal.** A node is a *noise node* iff, measured with **30 seeds per condition** (training, split and joint seeds; `"vary"` split), it has **≥ 2 material edits** (resolved, \|Δ̂_J\| > 2 se, and \|Δ̂_J\| ≥ 0.002) on the standard E1 edit set, with **mean plug-in P(wrong) ≥ 0.10** over those edits, and σ_joint > 0. The seed count is fixed at 30 for every node, so the rule's selection cap is the same everywhere. The seed-bootstrap probability of qualifying is reported but is **not** part of the decision. Library versions (Python, scikit-learn, numpy) are recorded in every measurement manifest. |
| D21 uniform application (22 Oct) | The rule applies to **every** node, including the five 18 Oct D21 nodes, which are re-measured at 30 seeds in the same environment as the candidates. A node that fails is removed from the noise-node pool: it does not count towards the ≥ 8 target and does not enter the C3 gate; it may remain an engineering node. No exception after the results are seen. |
| D21 pool (22 Oct) | `openml_credit_approval` **added** (qualified at 30 seeds on 22 Oct; it is re-checked in the common run). `openml_diabetes` **dropped** (no material edit at 30 seeds; not re-measured). New candidates, measured together with the others: `openml_credit_g` (OpenML 31), `openml_sonar` (40), `openml_blood_transfusion` (1464), `openml_qsar_biodeg` (1494); IDs checked on openml.org on 22 Oct. Not chosen: `ilpd` (1480; same risk profile as diabetes); `climate-model-simulation-crashes` (1467) is the reserve. Data caches are fetched on the author's Mac (the VMs have no openml.org access). Optional, later: one further MLP-type node for diversity (six of the eight target nodes share the OpenML template's edit space). |
| D21 common measurement (22 Oct) | One 30-seed E1 run over all ten nodes (5 original + `credit_approval` + 4 candidates; "nine" in the first version of this row was a counting error, corrected 22 Oct), CPU only, one environment, accepted by the coordinating session before merging. This run is the C1 noise-floor table. |

| R1-E0-PROTOCOL (26 Oct) | **E0 protocol fix before any Phase 5 evidence run** (spec §5.5, v2): (a) the width slope regresses the predicted std on the **label's own std** (the binned soft label's std with the same bin representatives); the label's own slope vs s is reported as the ceiling; the same reference applies to A4. (b) **Train to convergence**: validation cells held out of the training grid, early stopping, at most 2,000 steps, the same budget for every model. (c) A **fresh LayerNorm + linear belief head at lr 1e-3 for every model**; released heads are reported separately. The DRYRUN is re-run on Kev-0.8B and Qwen-0.8B under v2 and reported; **the author then sets the E0 and A4 thresholds in a PLAN row** and writes the Phase 5 authorization. |

| R1-RPS-NORM (26 Oct) | RPS is normalised by **1/(K−1)** (spec §6.1). |
| R1-EDGES-10 (26 Oct) | **10 finite bin edges**: the cumulative head has 10 threshold sigmoids (plus the no-op Bernoulli) and the Jev rung asks **10 yes/no questions**; the spec's "11" is corrected. |

*Implementation choices by the Track P agent under these rows (items 1 and 3 — token-row lr, validation cells, evaluation every 100, lr reuse, frozen Qwen rows — confirmed by R1-TRACKP-CHOICES; the 2,000-step stage cap is replaced by R1-E0-BUDGET and the A4 threshold set by R1-E0-THRESHOLDS; the rest stays the agent's reading; `docs/r1/reports/TRACKP_E0V2_DRYRUN_20261026.md`):* (1) token rows at lr 1e-4 — 10× below the new heads, but not below the top of the adapter grid; (2) the lr grid also applies to released heads (Kev's pointer head, Laya's decision head), which train at the adapter lr; (3) E0 v2: validation cells (1,3), (3,2), (5,4), (8,2), (0,3), (9,3); evaluation every 100 steps, patience 5 evaluations, the starting model counted as a candidate; the 2,000-step cap applies to each stage (head fit and LoRA, so ≤ 4,000 steps in all); the lr is chosen on the fresh-head soft run and reused for the hard-label contrast and the native-readout run; the Qwen control's new token rows stay frozen in the fresh-head run; (4) A4 v2 criterion form: predicted std non-decreasing wherever the label's std increases (plus the median per-prompt slope on the label std); (5) A3 budget = 1,024 − the longest rendering of the prompt over the pool families, levels filled to the word.

W is the largest of {8, 10, 12, 20} that binds in ≥ 50 % of pilot campaigns on both nodes, else 20. <br>(c) δ_Y = max(0.002, 0.5 σ_node) in raw metric units (digits 0.0033, tabular 0.0020). This is a declared departure from PLAN:118's normalisation. <br>(d) `vary` split for every Track S run, a declared departure from the MLP nodes' D21 `fixed`. <br>(e) E2 verify off for every Track S policy. <br>(f) Duplicates consume a trial and an LLM call (the deployment rule). <br>(g) The incumbent-error model (T4 revision R1) is not needed by Track S and stays open for the proof check. <br>(h) Primary nodes: digits_mlp on TASL-2 and tabular_mlp on TASL-7. **n = 300 fresh instances per node** (rev 7.2). ### R1-CACHEPROMPT-FALSE (31 Oct 2026, author) — accepted

| Row | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-CACHEPROMPT-FALSE (31 Oct) | **llama-server's prompt cache is the cause of Track S's S0.1 non-identity, and `cache_prompt` is now explicit.** D6 F1/F2 isolated the confounds (cold control 0.930/0.870 = S0.1; omitting the field ≡ `true`; server count no effect; warmth explained the 30 Oct control); `false` gives 1.000/1.000 on both hosts. The **harness** `llamacpp` provider sends `cache_prompt` explicitly on every request, default **false**, configured by `LLAMACACHE_PROMPT`→`LLAMACPP_CACHE_PROMPT`, and every manifest and `llm_call` record stores the value used; pre-change records are labelled "absent (= true, llama-server default)". Independent review accepted the change (**round 2**) at harness `a3fedbbd44309b3fdf99d853ad841b9ec2c299e2` (branch `cacheprompt-20261031`, **not merged into `r1`**) with paper pin `d10b05b738fc0e0dba54e0e692c248bedf095482`. The author's ruling file is `docs/prompts/TRACKS_CACHEPROMPT_FIX_PROMPT_20261031.md`. Cost: ≈640 evaluated prompt tokens/call and +6.06 % (digits) / +6.27 % (tabular) mean latency versus the cached mode. A full S0.1 re-run under the new pins is authorised; `k` stays unset and Phase 3 does not start until it passes. |

### 3 November 2026 — main proposer (author decision; recorded by an AI agent, Claude, chat session)

The author chose option 3 of the chat discussion of 3 Nov ("我选3，非thinking模式"): the C3 training data and the main closed-loop tables come from the proposer that the Track X exam selects, run with thinking off. Items marked *(proposed)* are the drafting session's recommendations and need the author's confirmation before an operator relies on them.

| Decision | Author decision |
|---|---|
| R1-MAIN-PROPOSER (3 Nov; revised 4 Nov after independent review) | **The main proposer of Paper B is the Track X pick, run with thinking off.** This replaces D15's `qwen2.5-coder:7b` as the main manager, and X.7(a)'s use of the pick as a D22 deployment check only. <br><br>**(a) Selection, by a new frozen rule.** <br>• The exam's rule `r1/exam/rules/x5_rule_v1.yaml` admits always-thinking models (ranked on their always-on μ̂) and picks the B2 mode on a sensitivity node; `analyze.b2_summary` ignores the mode. It is **replaced before B1 (X.4) starts** by `x5_rule_v2`. <br>• v2 eligibility: Stage A pass, D28 pass, **and able to run with thinking off** (a non-thinking model, or a declared `off_switch`). <br>• v2 finalists: the top 4 eligible models by **thinking-off** μ̂, plus the best eligible model of an absent family. <br>• v2 runs B2 **with thinking off** for every finalist. <br>• The pick is the best thinking-off B2 audited Y; ties go to lower GPU-h. <br>• Matching code changes in `r1/exam/analyze.py` (`load_rule`, `b1_summary`, `b2_summary`) and `r1/scripts/exam_runner.py` (B1/B2 modes); the rule's mode fields are quoted strings. <br>• v2, the analyze change and an independent review are committed with the rule's sha256 in a PLAN row **R1-EXAM-RULE-X5V2** (the id contains `R1-EXAM-RULE`, as `runtime.author_gate` requires) before the B1 authorization. Draft: `proposals/X5_RULE_V2_DRAFT_20261104.md`. <br>• If B1 nevertheless runs under v1, the pick was **not** chosen by a rule frozen before data, and this is disclosed. <br><br>**(b) Main C3 data = sweep 2 (RUNBOOK B.1b)**, run with the pick. <br>• Same as sweep 1: the eight nodes of `authorizations/c3_sweep1_20261027.md`, 18 instances, W = 20, memory `append_only_summary` and `none`, `vary` split, structure-only `manager_proposal/v1`, temperature 0.2 with the D26 seed. <br>• The same instance seeds as sweep 1. They are fixed per (node, k) and are also the in-campaign `train_seed` / `split_seed`. <br>• Thinking off at the server, exactly as in the exam: `--chat-template-kwargs {"enable_thinking":false}` from the registry's `thinking_policy`, with `--jinja` and q8_0 KV cache. <br>• `cache_prompt: false` on every call. <br>• Audits: K = 10, seeds 7001–7005 for labels and 7006–7010 for evaluation, under the R1-SWEEP1-CLASSIFY and R1-B2-DECOUPLE rules. <br><br>**(c) What differs from sweep 1, beyond the manager**, so the cross-proposer contrast is proposer **plus** stack: <br>• sweep 1 ran `cache_prompt` at the default (R1-SWEEP1-CACHE-DISCLOSURE); <br>• the same llama.cpp commit (620d222), built for sm_52 on a TITAN X against sm_89 on a 4090; <br>• the CPU host of the in-campaign worker runs; <br>• context 8,192 against 16,384; <br>• default KV cache against q8_0; <br>• `--jinja` and the thinking flag; <br>• the harness pin (`6adf385` against the next pin, which carries the accepted `cache_prompt` fix of harness branch `cacheprompt-20261031` @ `a3fedbb` — explicit `cache_prompt` on every request, default false; R1-CACHEPROMPT-FALSE, 31 Oct, on `trackS-ops-20261030` — with its Track S ancestry from `115df37`, and any R1-HARNESS-NEXT-PIN fixes) and the paper commit; a check that the harness defaults used by the sweep are unchanged is part of prerequisite 3. <br>All of these are listed in every cross-proposer table. <br><br>**(d) Sweep 1 becomes the second source.** <br>• It stays frozen (R1-B1-CORPUS-FREEZE) and is audited under the existing B.2 rules. <br>• It replaces B.6's "second manager". The node extension of B.6 stays optional. <br><br>**(e) Training, splits and leakage.** <br>• The B.7 gate, the A5/A6 evidence checkpoints and the C/D tables use sweep 2. Sweep 1 is added as a stratum. Choosing pooled training over sweep 2 alone is fixed on validation before any test read *(proposed)*. <br>• Twin campaigns share instance seeds, training and split seeds, and audit seeds, so the same (instance, incumbent, edit) gets **the same label in both sources**. Therefore: <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;1. both sources use **one instance → train/dev/test assignment**; <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;2. the cross-proposer table goes from one source's **train** partition to the other's **test** partition only; <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;3. duplicates are grouped across sources by (instance, incumbent hash, edit hash) for folds and for deduplication; <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;4. pooled intervals use an **instance-cluster** bootstrap. <br>• Until sweep 2 exists, Track P may run its pipeline on sweep-1 labels as ENGINEERING_ONLY, with label seeds 7001–7005 only and never 7006–7010 (R1-SWEEP1-CLASSIFY). <br><br>**(f) Hosts. Amends R1-SWEEP-HOST.** <br>• Sweep 2 runs on **one RTX 4090 host for the whole sweep** (TASL-2 or TASL-7, chosen by the author). <br>• A labelled table may combine sweep 1 (deepthought) and sweep 2 only with **source as a stratum**. Within each source, one host. <br>• Sweep 2's fresh-seed audits run on deepthought's CPUs like B.2 *(proposed)*. If a transferred batch cannot be audited there, the audits run on the sweep host, and 50 proposals are audited on both hosts. <br>• A4, C.2, C.3 and D.3 move to 4090 hosts wherever they run the main proposer (planned in Phase C). <br><br>**(g) Order.** <br>1. `x5_rule_v2` (R1-EXAM-RULE-X5V2) → B1 → B2 → X.7. <br>2. Sweep-1 B.2 complete, as R1-HARNESS-NEXT-PIN requires, or an explicit author exception. <br>3. The harness pin carrying the `cache_prompt` switch, and the paper-side sweep change passing the thinking policy and server flags, both reviewed. <br>4. D28 liveness of the pick on the eight nodes, which also re-measures prompt lengths with the pick's tokenizer. <br>5. Projection, then the author's authorization note, then sweep 2, then audits. <br>Phase B's 22 Nov target slips. <br><br>**(h) Cost.** <br>• Under v2, B2 runs only thinking-off rows, so its GPU time is at most v1's: about 5 finalists × a few GPU-h. <br>• Always-thinking models are not eligible. Running them in B1 only for the appendix costs several GPU-h each: B1 has about 240 proposals per model, and Stage A measured about 58 s per request for these models, so about 4 h each. *(Proposed: they skip B1, and the appendix reports them from Stage A.)* <br>• Before sweep 2's authorization, the operator projects calls × the pick's thinking-off latency plus runs × CPU time ÷ workers. Above 4 days on one 4090 is a STOP for the author *(proposed)*. <br><br>**(i) Disclosure.** The three exam nodes are among the eight sweep nodes. Instances and seeds are disjoint (exam 9001+ and 9101+/9201+, sweeps 7001–7010 and the sweep instance seeds), so no label leaks. Absolute closed-loop Y of the pick still carries a selection (winner's-curse) effect, so results lead with paired contrasts. <br><br>**(j) Track S** keeps its pinned `qwen2.5-coder:7b` manager (R1-TRACKS-HOST/-2 on the branch `docs/trackS-decisions-30oct`, not yet merged here), and its result is stated for the loop with that proposer. Whether to repeat it with the pick is decided after its outcome *(proposed)*. |

### 4 November 2026 — benchmark expansion and plan revisions (author adoption; recorded by an AI agent, Claude, chat session)

The author adopted `docs/r1/proposals/PAPER_B_REVIEW_BENCH_EXPANSION_20261104.md` (the "review document") **in full** on 4 Nov 2026: all of its decisions D1–D14 (§6) at their recommended defaults, and its revisions in §§2–5 and 8. **Evidence of adoption.** The author supplied the document as a chat attachment on 4 Nov 2026 (01:29). Its status line, as supplied, reads "ADOPTED IN FULL by the author, 4 Nov 2026 (chat): all of D1–D14 in section 6, each at its recommended default, and the revisions in sections 2–5 and 8". With it the author wrote: "我发现现行计划中存在很多问题以及漏洞，特别是实验集以及后面后训练的细节，起草了这个文档说明问题。你认真阅读，并对我们的现行计划，runbook以及一切相关文档进行修正修改". The rows below record that adoption in the document's words. The author confirms or corrects them by committing them (or by an explicit chat instruction to commit them).

- **Ids.** The document's D1–D14 are recorded as **R1-BX-D1 … R1-BX-D14**, because the ids D1–D28 already name the problem-spec decisions (§10 above).
- **Proposed items.** Text marked *(proposed)* is the recording session's operationalisation of an adopted decision. It is not part of the adopted text and binds nothing until the author confirms it.
- **Open items.** Text marked *(open)* is a question the review document leaves open or a conflict the recording session found. The author decides it.
- **Conditions the document sets on the coordinating agent.** (i) R1-BX-D3 takes effect only if its check passes; if it fails, K stays 10 and the agent STOPs for the author. (ii) R1-BX-D8 is committed before any sweep-2 label is analysed. (iii) The R1-BX-D14 dates are targets, recomputed from measured throughput after the T1 pilot; changes are reported to the author before they are applied. Under the 4 Nov two-agent split the "coordinating agent" is **the Mac-local agent**; the operator supplies the measured throughput.

| Decision | Author decision (4 Nov 2026; adopted from the review document) |
|---|---|
| R1-BX-D1 (4 Nov) | **Role of GPU tasks.** Part of the GPU tasks become **label nodes** (tier T1: they train C3 and enter the gate); the rest are evaluation only (tier T2). The **primary generalisation test is leave-one-suite-out**: train on two suites, test on the third. Leave-one-node-out within the CPU pool becomes secondary. *Supersedes* R1-TRACKG-ROLE's "G.4 role is evaluation/transfer only" (its validation-only selection for test-scored tasks stands) and §9 item 5's "leave-one-benchmark-out" wording. |
| R1-BX-D2 (4 Nov) | **Benchmark scope.** 12–16 real tasks from three suites — autoresearch (1 task), 1GC-7RC (7 tasks), MLGym (13 tasks) — covering ≥ 5 domains (LM, vision, graph, tabular, time series, text classification, RL). Variants of one task do not count toward the total. MLAgentBench is added as a fourth suite only if cheap. The 8 CPU nodes stay as **tier T0, the controlled testbed**: theory validation, Track S, ablations and the low-interaction condition (R1-BX-D4). Tiers, from review document §3: <br>• **T0** — the current 8 CPU nodes. <br>• **T1, label + evaluation** — autoresearch (short fixed-step variant); 1GC-7RC lighter tasks: TinyShakespeare, Forest Cover, ETTh1, AG News, possibly ogbg-molhiv; MLGym lighter tasks: CIFAR-10, Fashion-MNIST, House Price, and a MinAtar/MountainCar-type RL task (to be checked against the MLGym task table in the census). <br>• **T2, evaluation only** — 1GC-7RC TinyImageNet and VOC; optionally MLAgentBench light tasks (cifar10, imdb, ogbn-arxiv). <br>• **Not used** — RE-Bench and MLE-bench (hours per run, cannot be audited); DACBench (a different problem type). <br>**Integration principle (review document §3).** The suites' **task assets** (data, baseline script, evaluator) become harness nodes. Their agent frameworks are not used: our harness is the loop, so every node shares one runner, one provenance record and one audit path. <br>*Supersedes* R1-FOCUSED-SCOPE's "defer MLGym unless a container is available by 10 Nov", R1-TRACKG-SCOPE (Track G = autoresearch plus MLGym; 1GC-7RC removed) and R1-BENCH-HARD's role and membership wording where they differ. <br>*(Settled 4 Nov 12:45 by R1-1GC-FALLBACK: MLAgentBench replaces 1GC-7RC.)* *(open)* R1-TRACKG-SCOPE removed 1GC-7RC because the repository named on arXiv 2605.17046 was not public. The census (R1-BX-REVISIONS (e)) checks whether it is public now. If it is not, this is a STOP for the author: rebuild the tasks from the paper's description and public datasets (disclosed as not the suite's own assets), or make MLAgentBench the third suite. Leave-one-suite-out needs three suites. |
| R1-BX-D3 (4 Nov; **conditional**) | **Audit seeds K = 6** (3 label + 3 evaluation seeds), with the soft-label width taken from each node's own σ (s = σ̂_node/√K_label). **It takes effect only if the sweep-1 subsampling check passes:** sweep-1 K = 10 audits are subsampled to K = 6, and the stability of labels and of the gate is checked (CPU only, a few hours). **If the check fails, K stays 10 and the coordinating agent STOPs for the author.** Until the check has passed and its result is recorded in a PLAN row, K = 10 applies everywhere. <br>*Dependency (found while recording).* Sweep-1 B.2 audits do not exist yet (B.2 is at STOP), so the check can run only after B.2 (operator O4). Until then T1 audit costs are planned at both K = 6 and K = 10. <br>*(proposed)* **Scope:** K = 6 applies to T1 audits, where the GPU cost lies. T0 audits (sweeps 1 and 2 on the CPU nodes) stay at K = 10, because they cost CPU minutes and their design is frozen in R1-MAIN-PROPOSER (b). Tables that mix tiers carry the per-row K_label in the soft-label width. <br>*(proposed)* **Check protocol** (drafted by the Mac agent, reviewed, and committed by the author before it runs): <br>• it uses the **label seeds 7001–7005 only**: every 3-subset is compared with all five; <br>• pass if (i) the sign of the action target u at K_label = 3 agrees with the K_label = 5 sign on ≥ 90 % of labelled proposals, and (ii) the median absolute difference of μ is ≤ 0.25 in z units; <br>• the expected noise-only widening is √(5/3) ≈ 1.29. <br>*(open)* A **gate-stability** criterion would need the evaluation seeds 7006–7010 and arms trained on sweep-1 labels. That use is not covered by R1-B2-DECOUPLE, R1-SWEEP1-CLASSIFY or R1-MAIN-PROPOSER (e) (sweep 1 and sweep 2 share instances and labels). It runs only if the author grants an explicit exception, and only after R1-BX-D8-CONFIRM. |
| R1-BX-D4 (4 Nov) | **Edit schema on GPU nodes.** GPU nodes take **code-level diffs** (autoresearch already edits any part of `train.py`). CPU nodes keep the single-parameter schema as an explicit **low-interaction condition**. The two conditions then form a contrast on the NF condition ("an edit's increment does not depend on earlier keeps") instead of a confound. The S1.1 edit–edit interaction measurement runs on the GPU nodes, and per-node interaction strength is reported as a covariate ("distance from NF"). **Harness changes needed:** diff application; inert and duplicate detection for diffs; provenance. They are drafted, reviewed and pinned **before any GPU sweep**. <br>*(proposed)* A new proposal schema version (`manager_proposal/v2-diff`), with each node's `editable_paths` / `frozen_paths` as the scope and the existing scope validator enforcing it. The CPU-node schema stays `manager_proposal/v1`. <br>*(open)* The main proposer was selected on the single-parameter schema (Track X). Its competence with diffs is first measured by D28 liveness on the T1 nodes. A pick that fails diff liveness on a T1 node is a STOP for the author. |
| R1-BX-D5 (4 Nov) | **Label budget.** Label runs use a **fixed step or token budget**, not wall-clock time, and **one GPU model per table** (consistent with the 24 Oct "no mixing hosts within one table" rule, R1-SWEEP-HOST). Label runs are short (1–2 min at the fixed budget). Closed-loop evaluation may use longer fixed budgets (about 5 min). |
| R1-BX-D6 (4 Nov) | **Second proposer.** One **API proposer**, in **closed-loop evaluation only**, on T1/T2 nodes. Preferably not DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, which writes the R-B warm-up demonstrations (R1-RB-TEACHER). Only Track S needs seed-replayable sampling; closed-loop evaluation does not. <br>*(open)* Which API model; its version pin and request log (as for the Jev rung, R1-JEV-RUNG); the host the calls go from, under the secrets rule; and the cost cap. |
| R1-BX-D7 (4 Nov) | **Practitioner baselines** in the main table: <br>• **Karpathy default** — always execute, single run, keep if Δ̂ > 0; <br>• **fixed-r replication + test** — racing style, keep by a t-test at a declared level. <br>They are added to the ladder if absent. <br>*(proposed)* If A0 (ρ) is exactly the Karpathy default, A0 is relabelled as such in the tables rather than duplicated. Fixed-r uses r = 3 and a one-sided Welch t-test at α = 0.05, and is charged for its replicate runs at matched compute. |
| R1-BX-D8 (4 Nov; **commit before any sweep-2 label is analysed**) | **C3 gate statistic.** **Replaces the 18 Oct C3 gate's per-node criterion** ("reaches δ_n on at least two nodes"). The primary criterion is the **across-node aggregate: the IQM of gap closed and the probability of improvement, with node-stratified bootstrap CIs**. The per-node table is secondary. The gate is read on CPU + T1 labels (R1-BX-D14). <br>**Implementation of the adopted condition "committed before any sweep-2 labels are analysed": a separate author row `R1-BX-D8-CONFIRM`**, carrying the confirmed numbers and the sha of the gate code, is committed **before any sweep-2 (T0 or T1) label table is copied back for analysis or read by any analysis code**. Every reader of those tables (`dataset` build of evaluation columns, offline evaluation, the gate, the Track P loaders) refuses to run without a PLAN line containing `R1-BX-D8-CONFIRM`. <br>*(proposed operationalisation, to be confirmed in R1-BX-D8-CONFIRM):* <br>• per node, gap closed = (U_arm − U_A2) / (U_oracle − U_A2) on test instances with `y_eval`. U_oracle is the noisy-oracle ceiling of TRACK_P §7.1. U is the one-step utility scaled to a 20-run campaign; <br>• the IQM is taken across nodes; <br>• probability of improvement: per node, the share of test instances where the arm's utility exceeds A2's (ties count ½), averaged over nodes; <br>• bootstrap: 2,000 resamples of instances within each node, seed fixed; <br>• nodes whose denominator U_oracle − U_A2 is below δ_n (D18) on the dev split have no defined gap closed: they are excluded from the IQM, listed, and kept in the probability of improvement; <br>• **pass** if, for A3 or A5 with Holm over the two arms at family-wise one-sided α = 0.05 (the first-ranked arm tested with one-sided 97.5 % lower bounds, the second with 95 %), the lower bound of the IQM gap closed is > 0 **and** the lower bound of the probability of improvement is > 0.5. The point IQM gap closed is reported against a practical size of 0.1. <br>*(open)* Pooling T0 (CPU) and T1 (GPU) labels in one gate is a deliberate cross-host table; R1-SWEEP-HOST's no-mixing rule applies within each tier, with tier as a stratum. |
| R1-BX-D9 (4 Nov) | **Self-control arm and wording.** The self-control arm of the three-way comparison (self-control vs trained vs hybrid) is **A4: the Track X pick, zero-shot, isotonic-calibrated**. It does not depend on R-B. **R-B becomes an optional "trained self-control" ablation.** In the paper only R-B is called RL; R-A is called **"decision-focused fine-tuning"**. *Amends* TRACK_P §6.4 ("R-B is the LLM self-control arm"), RUNBOOK H.7/D.2, and R1-FOCUSED-SCOPE / R1-TRACKP-FOCUS / R1-RB-GRPO's "R-B conditional": R-B is now optional, and the comparison no longer needs it. |
| R1-BX-D10 (4 Nov) | **Track S scope.** Add **1–2 GPU nodes** to Track S: autoresearch (short fixed-step variant) and one RL-type task with high noise and interacting edits. **Fallback date ≈ 15 Dec 2026:** if Track S has no campaign-level result by then, the DAC-reduction argument rests on the theorem plus synthetic controls, and the paper says so explicitly. <br>*(open)* Which manager Track S uses on GPU nodes. R1-MAIN-PROPOSER (j) keeps `qwen2.5-coder:7b` for Track S on the CPU nodes; diffs on GPU nodes (R1-BX-D4) may need another model. The GPU-node design is a Track S design revision, reviewed and committed before any GPU-node Track S run. |
| R1-BX-D11 (4 Nov) | **Cuts.** **B.6 (CPU-node extension) is cut**, replaced by the T1 GPU nodes. **Stage D (DAgger, C.2) is one round on 1–2 T1 nodes only.** **R-B is optional** (R1-BX-D9). |
| R1-BX-D12 (4 Nov) | **Screening transparency.** Report the screening funnel (screened / qualified per suite, under D21 and D28). Evaluate the non-qualifying nodes in an appendix, to show that the controller does not hurt when noise is low (it should approach greedy). |
| R1-BX-D13 (4 Nov) | **TabPFN comparison.** Add an **information-matched** comparison. A shared feature view: the hand features plus a proposal-text embedding reduced by PCA. TabPFN and Kev run once under equal information, in addition to their native inputs. TabPFN runs in **regression mode**, with its predictive distribution binned, to avoid the classification class cap (the cap is verified for the version used). *Amends* R1-TABPFN: the native rows stay. <br>*(open)* The text-embedding model and its pin, the PCA dimension (fixed on training data only), and how Kev receives the matched view (the same features serialised as text, without the free proposal text). |
| R1-BX-D14 (4 Nov; **targets**) | **Dates.** B.7 ≈ **8 Dec 2026**, on CPU + T1 labels with the aggregate criterion (R1-BX-D8); D.3 closed loop ≈ **3 Jan 2027**; manuscript **10 Jan 2027** unchanged, with writing in parallel from early December; Track S fallback ≈ **15 Dec 2026** (R1-BX-D10). **Track G becomes the critical path and starts now**, not after B.7. GPU-node noise floors and liveness start on Ada while Track X holds TASL-2. These dates are targets: the coordinating agent recomputes them from measured throughput after the T1 pilot and reports changes to the author before applying them. *Supersedes* R1-INTERNAL-FREEZES' B.7 26 Nov and D.3 20 Dec; its labels date of 3 Nov has already passed. |
| R1-BX-REVISIONS (4 Nov) | **Other adopted revisions (review document §§2–5, 8).** <br>**(a) P1.** The CPU pool is demoted to the controlled testbed T0 (theory validation, cheap Track S, ablations). The main tables move to real tasks. <br>**(b) P3.** The harness work for diffs, the per-node interaction covariate and the NF contrast, as in R1-BX-D4. <br>**(c) P12.** Noise is estimated per GPU node. **Divergence** is recorded as a separate outcome class and not folded into the mean. *(proposed)* Divergence = a non-finite loss, or a run that ends without a metric at the fixed budget; a divergent proposal gets no z-value and enters no bin, and its divergence count is reported. *(open)* How a divergent proposal enters the belief target and the action target u (for example as a separate outcome bin, or as a cost), and what happens when only some audit seeds diverge. Each node's soft-label width uses that node's own σ̂. *(proposed)* If a GPU node's noise floor is heavy-tailed, the node's soft label uses a Student-t, and the choice is declared before labels are built. <br>**(d) P13.** For causal decision models the proposal is placed **after** the history, immediately before `<decide>`. An **E6 probe** compares history-first with proposal-first. <br>**(e) Census (§3).** Per candidate task, the server agent records: run time at the fixed budget on each GPU model; VRAM; determinism; a 10-seed noise floor on 3–5 representative edits; the D21 verdict; D28 liveness with the main proposer. **Ada first.** *(open)* It also records whether each suite's repository and licence permit use of its task assets. <br>**(f) Cost and hosts (§4).** Rough costs, to be calibrated by a pilot: about 40 GPU-h per T1 node at 2-minute runs and K = 6 (noise floor about 2, sweep-2 executions about 8, audits of about 150 proposals about 30); about 400 GPU-h for 10 T1 nodes, about 600 with T2 closed-loop evaluation. At K = 10 the audit share rises to about 50 GPU-h per node. ≈ 150 audited proposals per T1 node. Hosts: <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;• the 2 × RTX 4090 are partly available; Track X holds TASL-2 until about 15 Nov; <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;• deepthought (4 × TITAN 12 GB, exclusive, slower): **a model may be served across two TITANs, so the main proposer may run there and free 4090 time for task runs and Track P**. This *amends* R1-MAIN-PROPOSER (f) ("one RTX 4090 host for the whole sweep"). *(proposed)* Sweep 2 may run its proposer on deepthought across two TITANs only if the dual-card qualification (operator O5) passes against a pass criterion the author commits; within one source, and within one tier, the proposer runs on one serving configuration. *(open)* Whether sweep 2's T0 and T1 parts must use the same proposer host; <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;• Ada (8 × RTX 6000 Ada, shared, interruptible): resumable work only (the sweep already resumes per root); <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;• one GPU model per label table (R1-BX-D5). <br>*(open)* For T1 nodes the proposer host and the task host differ. Which GPU model runs the T1 label runs, and therefore which host's noise floor sets σ̂, is the author's choice after the census. A noise floor measured on Ada sets σ̂ only for a table whose runs are on Ada. <br>**(g) Containers (§8).** Order of preference: (1) avoid containers and port task assets natively into harness venvs, isolating code diffs with a per-run working copy and resource limits; (2) container-dependent work on deepthought, where the author is in the `docker` group, or on the 4090 hosts if Docker or Podman is available there; (3) on deepthought2, use what exists without admin help; (4) if deepthought2 is still needed, ask for rootless Podman (subuid/subgid entries for `dwong`) or Apptainer, never `docker` group membership. **Apptainer is the container route where Docker is unavailable**: Docker images are pulled rootless (`apptainer pull <name>.sif docker://<image>`) and run with `apptainer exec --nv`; images are read-only (`--writable-tmpfs` or a bound per-run working copy); `--containall` or `--cleanenv`; `APPTAINER_CACHEDIR` and `APPTAINER_TMPDIR` under `/ceph/dwong/`; the `.sif` sha256 is recorded in provenance like a GGUF. The author's 4 Nov check found `/usr/bin/apptainer` and `/usr/bin/singularity`, no Podman and no subuid/subgid entries for `dwong`, on a host still to be named. *(open)* The author confirms by `hostname` whether that host was deepthought or deepthought2; if it was deepthought, the check is repeated on deepthought2. deepthought2 remains outside the run hosts unless a row adds it. <br>**(h) Routing (§7).** Mac-local work goes to the Claude on the Mac (`prompts/MAC_LOCAL_RUNBOOK_20261104.md`); all server experiments go to the single SSH operator (`prompts/OPERATOR_RUNBOOK_20261104.md`), **including porting the suites' task assets into harness nodes**. *(proposed)* For that porting the operator holds a scoped exception to its "never edit code" rule: it writes new node assets only (node specs, data preparation, fixed-step wrappers, metric parsers, the declared representative edits), on its own branch, each reviewed before use; harness core code (diff support, label code, the gate) stays with the Mac agent. Every phase keeps the protocol: an independent fresh agent reviews, and the executing agent fixes. |

**Rows these decisions supersede or amend** (each older row stays as history): R1-BENCH-HARD (membership and role); R1-FOCUSED-SCOPE (MLGym deferral, B.6 deferral, R-B conditional); R1-INTERNAL-FREEZES (B.7 and D.3 dates); R1-TRACKG-ROLE (evaluation/transfer only) and R1-TRACKG-SCOPE (Track G membership); the 18 Oct C3 gate (per-node criterion, replaced by R1-BX-D8); R1-TRACKP-FOCUS and R1-RB-GRPO ("R-B conditional" becomes optional); R1-TABPFN (adds the matched comparison); R1-MAIN-PROPOSER (f) (proposer host); and the B.6 entries of R1-MAIN-PROPOSER (d) and RUNBOOK B.6 (cut).

- *(open)* domain coverage: time series was covered only by ETTh1, now an extra task; MLAgentBench cifar10 (and possibly house-price) nearly duplicate MLGym tasks, so the census should prefer non-duplicating tasks.

| ID | Author's decision |

| R1-B1-OFFLINE-LOADER-ONCE-08NOV | "不追认第一次为 PASS，按以下顺序处理：<br>1. 先做只读确认。 核对这 8 个文件当前的 SHA，和第一次日志里的前后值一致，不改动现场。<br>2. 修包装。 SSH 的退出码要可靠地保存下来，例如 ssh … ; rc=$?; echo &quot;SSH_EXIT=$rc&quot; \| tee -a &lt;log&gt;；如果用了管道，加 set -o pipefail，避免 ssh \| tee 只拿到 tee 的退出码。退出码写进日志，也写进单独的 rc 文件。<br>3. 只读重跑一次同样的离线缓存检查。判定通过需要三条同时满足：SSH_EXIT=0、日志里有 OFFLINE_LOADER_PASS、8 个文件前后 SHA 不变。<br>4. 第一次运行的日志和现场原样保留，报告中标为&quot;incomplete attempt: exit code not captured&quot;，不删除、不覆盖。<br>5. 由同一个独立审查验收重跑。验收前，B1 的其他门槛照旧，不因此放宽。<br>如果重跑结果和第一次不一致（加载失败或 SHA 变了），停下来报告，不要再重试。<br>还有一个顺带的好处：修好的包装可以复用到之后所有的 SSH 检查，后面不会再因为同样的原因卡住。" |
