After B1 run `fffa_20261111_b` stopped on an uncaught truncated proposer reply (report `reports/B1_STOP_FFFA_20261111.md`, branch `mac-b1-20261107` @ `8af27919`), the author ruled the STOP an engineering problem and set the route: diagnose the reasoning-budget support of the pinned llama.cpp and the exception path; record a truncated output as an invalid proposal with a per-call completion cap above the largest completion of the completed roots; a PLAN row with appendix disclosure; a new restart hand-off with run ID suffix `_c`. The author's message (11 Nov 2026) is not quoted; the row below is the Mac-local agent's record of it.

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-EXAM-B1-TRUNCATION-INVALID (11 Nov) | **A truncated proposer reply is an invalid proposal, and every proposer call carries a completion cap.** *STOP* (report above): at 13:51:09Z a `qwen36_27b` thinking-on reply (`openml_credit_approval` s9001, trial 13) filled the 32,768-token context (31,551 completion tokens, `--reasoning-budget 2048` configured); the OpenAI SDK's structured-output parser raised `LengthFinishReasonError`, the harness logged `llm_call_failed` and re-raised, and the root worker exited 1; 64 roots had completed. *Diagnosis:* `reports/B1_TRUNCATION_DIAGNOSIS_20261111.md` (exception path; `--reasoning-budget` in llama.cpp `620d222`; per-root completion usage from read-only host copies, `proposals/B1_TRUNCATION_HOST_CHECK_HANDOFF_20261111.md`). **Rule.** (1) A proposer reply cut short — finish reason `length` or `content_filter`, whether the SDK raises it or the reply is returned — is an **invalid proposal**: its text is discarded even if part of it would parse; it is recorded as an invalid trial (`parse_failed`, `PROPOSAL_PRECONDITION_FAILED`, no worker run); it is **charged to the LLM-call budget** like every proposal call; the campaign continues. Its `llm_call` record carries `truncated`, `finish_reason`, `max_completion_tokens` and the discarded character counts. Every other LLM-call failure remains a STOP. (2) Every proposer call carries **`max_completion_tokens = 16384`**. *Why 16,384:* fixed before the per-root usage of the stopped run was read; half the context, so a capped reply ends before the context limit for any prompt below 16k tokens (prompts ≈ 1.2k tokens; the largest is reported in the diagnosis); about eight times the configured 2,048-token thinking budget, leaving room for the JSON answer; a capped call ends in about 6 min at the 44 tokens/s measured in the STOP, against about 12 min to fill the context. *Time bound:* a root whose 80 LLM calls all end at the cap spends about 80 × 16,384 / r s on them, ≈ 8.3 h at 44 tokens/s but more than the unchanged 12 h per-root deadline (`root_timeout_seconds`, a STOP) below ≈ 30 tokens/s; the diagnosis reports r per candidate and mode. **Condition:** the cap must exceed the largest proposer completion of every completed root of `fffa_20261111_b`, checked root by root in the diagnosis report (`r1/scripts/b1_completion_usage.py`); if it does not, B1 does not restart and the author decides. The A4 observer call is not capped by this row (it already sends `max_tokens` 4096 and treats an unparseable answer as `query_failed`); its 60 s timeout is reported in the diagnosis (§5) and goes to the author if the margin is small. **Unchanged:** harness pin `a3fedbb`, thinking policy and `--reasoning-budget 2048`, prompts, schema, seeds, temperature, context, budgets (W = 20, LLM-call cap 80), nodes, `x5_rule_v2`, analysis, every other STOP, `R1-EXAM-B1-ANCHOR-R4/-H2`, and the root manifest and diagnostics (a truncated trial counts under `invalid` and `parse_failed`). **Code and re-pin:** `r1/exam/b1_llm_truncation.py` and `r1/exam/campaign.py::exam_manager` (`1334c616`, branch `mac-b1-trunc-20261111`, based on the run HEAD `fffa45a1`); `paper_commit` in `r1/scripts/configs/exam_b1_v2.json` moves from `fb57c6a4` to `1334c616` (`62adc6a3`). The re-pin is part of the fix the author ordered and needs no further author message. **Disclosure:** the appendix reports this as a post-hoc engineering change made after the stopped run's 64 roots and before any candidate result was read or used, with the cap and its reason, the number of truncated proposals per candidate and mode, and whether the 2,048-token thinking budget took effect in this build (draft `reports/B1_TRUNCATION_APPENDIX_DRAFT_20261111.md`). **Restart:** after independent review (`reviews/B1_TRUNCATION_REVIEW_20261111.md`), B1 restarts by `proposals/B1_RESTART_HANDOFF_C_20261111.md`: run ID `<first four hex of the new run HEAD>_<UTC date>_c`, a fresh output tree; run `fffa_20261111_b` and the run-6835 scene are not moved, changed or deleted. *One exception, proposed by the Mac-local agent and agreed by the author (message of 12 Nov 2026, not quoted):* the `_b` run's Track P marker `~/r1_locks/trackx_evidence.active` must be retired for a new handover (`b1_o2_marker.py create` uses exclusive create). It is a cooperative lock, not evidence; it is renamed into `~/r1_locks/_to_delete/`, not deleted; its content and receipts are recorded in the STOP report §2/§5; the precedent is `R1-EXAM-B1-ANCHOR-R4` (run 6835's marker at the `_b` restart). The receipts are kept (the retirement receipt in the new `_c` handover directory). B2, X.7 and sweep 2 remain separately gated. |

After B1 run `4b31_20261112_c` stopped at the proposal-integrity guard (report `reports/B1_STOP_4B31_20261112.md`, branch `mac-b1-20261107` @ `3b7d5704`), the author ruled the STOP an engineering problem. The author's route: diagnose 018/019 against 020, with a fixed-prompt replay run read-only on the host; inventory every model-output STOP on the B1 path; turn the model-output ones into invalid trials charged to the budget, with the proposal persisted before execution, internal inconsistencies still stopping, and a regression test per item; a PLAN row and a restart hand-off `_d`; assess, without adopting, the reuse of the 89 completed roots. The author's message (13 Nov 2026) is not quoted; the row below is the Mac-local agent's record of it.

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-EXAM-B1-MODEL-OUTPUT-INVALID (13 Nov) | **A B1 STOP or uncaught exception caused by a model output becomes an invalid trial; every proposal is journalled before execution.** *STOP* (report above): at 03:01:55Z, in `olmo31_32b` / `openml_credit_approval__s9002` trial 020, the worker guard found that the objective's directive and the structured edit differed, and stopped the batch after 89 completed roots. Trials 018/019 had asked for the same impossible `model_type` change with agreeing descriptions, were executed and were rejected by the node (`invalid_config`). *Diagnosis and inventory:* `reports/B1_MODEL_OUTPUT_DIAGNOSIS_20261113.md` (§1; the optional replay `proposals/B1_PROPOSAL_REPLAY_HANDOFF_20261113.md`; §3 inventory M1–M21). **Rule.** (1) Before the control plane can call the worker, each proposal headed for execution is checked against the worker guard's model-output conditions: directive missing; directive symbol not editable or different; old or new value different; backslash; `.py` value too long, not a literal, or not a finite number (M5–M9). A failing proposal is **not executed**, is recorded as an **invalid trial** (`PROPOSAL_PRECONDITION_FAILED`, no worker run, no observer call) and is **charged its LLM call**; the rejected edit and the reason are kept in the record (`b1_model_output_rejected`). The check is skipped for proposals the harness revises (D25 inactive key) or rejects by itself (its precondition checks), so every trial that did not STOP before is recorded exactly as before (two narrow exceptions, diagnosis §4.2a: a summary surrogate on a root's last proposer call, or on a proposal the D25 loop would have replaced, reached no later prompt under `_c` and is now an invalid trial). A rejection is final: the rejected proposal is not revised by the D25 loop. A `.py` value on which `ast.literal_eval` raises `TypeError` stays on its `_c` path (invalid trial after the worker starts). (2) llama-server's HTTP 400 for a prompt beyond the context is an invalid trial after trial 1, because the memory summary repeats model text; at trial 1 it stays a STOP (M19). A reply whose summary contains a lone surrogate, which would make every later request unencodable, is discarded and is an invalid trial (M20); the reply is parsed as the harness parses it (inline think blocks removed), and surrogates in other fields are left to the harness. (3) Every proposer call is journalled to `b1_proposals.jsonl` before the proposal is returned. Each row holds the objective, the structured edit, the parsed directive, the raw reply and the check outcome; the journal is validated against ledger and events in the root manifest and in `b1_batch`, and is in the evidence index. (4) **Still a STOP:** any proposal that reaches the worker guard and fails it (now an internal inconsistency), an edit path mismatch (M10), a replay failure of an executed edit (M16, now unreachable from model output), an executed configuration failing on an audit seed (M17), an observer timeout or transport failure (M15), an LLM transport failure (M18), the 12 h root deadline (M21), and all infrastructure, authority and other checks. M17 and M15 are listed for the author in the diagnosis §5, together with the unenforced `allowed_ranges` of `digits_mlp` (M14), which is left unchanged. **Unchanged:** harness pin `a3fedbb`, prompts, schema, seeds, temperature, completion cap, thinking policy, context, budgets (W = 20, LLM-call cap 80), nodes, `x5_rule_v2`, analysis, `R1-EXAM-B1-ANCHOR-R4/-H2` and `R1-EXAM-B1-TRUNCATION-INVALID`. **Code and re-pin:** `3458b672` and `02a91494` (branch `mac-b1-modelout-20261113`, based on the `_c` run HEAD `4b312844`); `paper_commit` moves from `1334c616` to `02a91494` (via `2eda52e7`, then `c5bf524b`). The re-pin is part of the fix the author ordered and needs no further author message. **Reuse of the 89 completed `_c` roots** (assessment only; diagnosis §6): scientifically identical under this rule, but bound to the `_c` authorization and lacking the journal evidence, so reuse would need a new reviewed import procedure. **Not adopted**; all roots are rerun. **Disclosure:** the appendix reports this as a post-hoc engineering change made after the stopped run's 89 roots, before any candidate result was used, with the rejection counts per candidate, mode and check, noting that rejections count toward the D28 invalid-trial rate and so can affect eligibility (draft `reports/B1_MODEL_OUTPUT_APPENDIX_DRAFT_20261113.md`). **Restart:** after independent review (`reviews/B1_MODEL_OUTPUT_REVIEW_20261113.md`), by `proposals/B1_RESTART_HANDOFF_D_20261113.md`. The run ID is `<first four hex of the new run HEAD>_<UTC date>_d`, with a fresh output tree. Runs `4b31_20261112_c`, `fffa_20261111_b` and `6835_20261108_a` are not moved, changed or deleted, except that the `_c` Track X marker is retired at restart, as the author ordered and as at the previous restarts (renamed into `~/r1_locks/_to_delete/`, receipts kept, the retirement receipt in the new `_d` handover directory). B2, X.7 and sweep 2 remain separately gated. |

### 15 November 2026 — B2 instance count (author)

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-EXAM-B2-POWER-PLAN | **B2 实例数按预先声明的精度规则确定：b2_instances_per_node = 4（种子 9011–9014）。** 这是精度规划，不就入围模型之间的区分作功效声明。规则见 `proposals/B2_POWER_PLAN_RULE_20261114.md`（sha256 `0294387c…`），在计算任何数字之前提交。估计量：每个 B2 节点上，入围模型 B2 最终审计 Y 的均值（种子 9201–9210，thinking off）；若入围 ≥ 2 个，则为两两的配对差。方差：取 B1 `_d` 全部 thinking-off 候选根的最终 Y 代理值，即最后一次被审计的 incumbent 在 9101–9104 上的平均指标；计算同一候选内、跨实例的合并方差，取单侧 80 % χ² 上限。种子噪声部分偏保守（B1 用 4 个种子，B2 用 10 个）；“最后被审计的 incumbent”的滞后偏差方向未知；合并方差假定各候选方差相同。gemma4_31b 自身的 sd 在 credit_approval 和 ionosphere 上高于合并上限，但用它自身的点估计仍得 n = 4。目标：每个节点双侧 95 % 置信区间半宽 ≤ 1 σ_node（σ_node 为 R1-C1-FLOOR 的节点噪声常数，即重要编辑的 sd_joint 中位数；`E1_20261022T214606Z/summary.json`，sha256 `eabc2f5c…`）。公式：n_j 为满足 t_{0.975,n−1}·√(k·V_j/n) ≤ σ_node,j 的最小 n（n ≥ 2；入围 1 个时 k = 1，≥ 2 个时 k = 2），n 取各节点 n_j 的最大值。每节点上限 10；超过 10 则停下交作者决定，不截断。结果：入围名单为 `[gemma4_31b]`（k = 1），n_j 依次为 4 / 3 / 3（digits_mlp / openml_credit_approval / openml_ionosphere），n = 4。审查员估算的保证概率（n = 4 时实现半宽 ≤ σ_node）：digits_mlp 为 0.85–0.91，另两个节点 ≥ 0.96。依据文件：脚本 `r1/scripts/b2_power_plan.py`（sha256 `c74a4252…`），输出 `reports/b2_power_plan_20261114/b2_power_plan.json`（sha256 `1236d805…`），B1 数据 `b1_manifests.json`（sha256 `dc5cd04d…`）。本行记录作者 11 月 14 日的指示：实例数由 Mac agent 按预先声明的规则从 B1 数据计算，每节点上限 10，超过则停下交作者决定；规则与数字定稿后由作者签字确认。 |

### 15 November 2026 — Track S ends at STOP 4 (author)

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-TRACKS-STOP4-END | **Track S 在 STOP 4 结束，不进入 EVIDENCE：不修改 A2-U，不重跑任何阶段；TASL-7 不再分配给 Track S。** 依据是两节点合并的 S0.5 摘要 `reports/TRACKS_S05_SUMMARY_20261115.md`（独立审查接受于 `74808c0a`）中的两项发现。(1) **校准门两节点都未通过**（design S0.5 (iii)：80 % 区间的覆盖率须在 [0.70, 0.90] 内，总体及每个 ≥ 20 条预测的分层都要满足）。digits_mlp（n = 298）：分层 0 为 0.538，1-2 为 0.674，≥ 3 为 0.944，总体 0.775。tabular_mlp（n = 330）：分层 0 为 0.368，1-2 为 0.653，≥ 3 为 0.831，总体 0.700。两节点的分层 0 都过低；按 runbook rev 3.3 Phase 4，这是交作者决定的 STOP，λ₂ 修正无法改变分层 0。(2) **E1 筛选不起作用。** 两节点拟合的先验中 τ0 都落在下限（0.15 σ_node），因为 τ_A2 < τ_w。因此 A2-U 对每个提议算出的 V\* 几乎相同（在全部合格的非重复 E1 决定上的极差：digits 0.036 σ_node，tabular 0.018 σ_node），并且远高于运行价格 g = 0.1 σ_node：V\* − g 在 digits 上为 2.58–2.61 σ_node，在 tabular 上为 4.04–4.06 σ_node。没有任何决定落在 g 或静态阈值（0.3 σ_node）以下或其 ±0.5 σ_node 之内；所有 E1 跳过都是规则判定的重复提议。两条 pilot 策略（a2u 与 static:0.3 σ_node,0）在两节点各 20 个实例上的动作序列逐条相同，截断到 W = 8/10/12/20 时结果也相同；S0.5b 中两条策略之间的 σ_D 及其 UCL 为 0，W = 40 时的 between_policy_sd_at_cap 也为 0。只有 E2 的 keep 是真实的决定。作者从摘要的三个选项中选 (c-iii)：不改 A2-U 的先验或方差结构，不调运行价格或预算，也不缩小为只比较 E2。此后的阶段均不进行，包括 S0.6、S0.7、S0.8 预声明、S0.9 授权、S3 和 S4，不产生 EVIDENCE。Track S 的全部数据（Phase 0–4，含 smoke、S0.1、S1.1、S0.4、S0.5 和 S0.5b）保持 ENGINEERING_ONLY，只在附录中作为说明 Track S 为何停止的 pilot 发现披露，不作为结果引用（RUNBOOK §1：ENGINEERING_ONLY/PILOT 运行不作为结果引用；草稿 `reports/TRACKS_PILOT_FINDINGS_APPENDIX_DRAFT_20261115.md`）：附录如实报告 A2-U 校准失败，不对 A2-U 本身作任何主张。Track S 在两台主机及 Mac 上留下的目录、进程、分支和数据原样保留，不移动、不修改、不删除（清单见 `reports/TRACKS_RESOURCES_AND_SCENES_20261115.md`）；如需清理，另由作者决定。本行记录作者 11 月 15 日的决定。 |

| R1-T4-PROOF-CHECK | **认可 Prop. 8′、10(a′)、10(c) 的主结论与证明（`proposals/T4_PROP10_REVISION_20261028.md` @ main `9ae6d877`），按下列勘误修订；R1 选 (ii)；R4 定为 20 个 proposal；§6 纳入五项离线检查，C4 强制分支检验延后。** 勘误：(1) 8′ 的单步证明不需要 U ⊥ W_{1:N}：把 §1 中这项假设移到 (NF) 条件下，并写明 8′ 无需它。(2) 8′.4：μ_n = 0 时 keep 与 discard 等价，但 g > 0 时 skip 严格优于 execute（原文"every decision ties"只对 g = 0 成立）；用 H_n = 0 识别 no-op 还需要非 no-op 的比较值在零点没有概率质量。(3) 10(a′) 三步证明成立；Step 1 从 G_n 缩到 O_n 用的是塔性质（原文称 weak union）；逐步上界可相加，因为 (NF) 使所有策略有同一提议序列分布，(NI) 使执行历史在给定提议历史后不改变当前后验。(4) 10(c)：精确最优性差距应写为 (J_probe − J_myopic)⁺；例如 α = 1、β = 0.1、g = 0.5、κ = 1 时直接相减约 −0.1011，实际差距为 0；原表四行差值都为正，脚本重跑与仓库输出逐字一致；论文保留存在性表述：即使 (NF) 成立，也存在 (NI) 失效且 myopic 损失任意大的例子。**R1 (ii)：** incumbent 误差 e 作为共享隐变量，8′ 的价值对 e 的后验取条件计算；实际 loop 不在 10(a′) 覆盖范围内，campaign 收益按实测报告。**R4：** gate 的放大 = 20 × 每个记录提议的加权平均效用，与 22 Oct 主分析（Run accounting in the 20-run scaling，option A）及 `r1/c3/offline_eval.py`（`CAMPAIGN_RUNS = 20`）一致；论文另注实际 sweep 按 20 次 worker run 设预算。**§6 纳入：** memory `none` 显示审计；重复提议计数（NF-c）；固定完整提议历史的 NI 遮挡检验；同一 incumbent 在位期间及 keep 后的残差检验（R1 机制）；已有配对上的 NF-b 交互检验。C4 强制分支检验延后。后果：Track S 附录可按每次决策的表述（8′ 第 2–3 部分）修改，不作 campaign 层面的结论（R1-TRACKS-STOP4-CONSEQUENCES 第 5 条）；T4 文件按本行勘误修订为一个独立审查的单元。 |

This convention applies on top of item (1); under it, item (1)'s resample clause has no further effect on the bound. This keeps R1-BX-D8's primary criterion (IQM of gap closed) and changes only how its interval is formed. **(5) per-tier test floor under P3:** T0 ≥ 9 and T1 ≥ 3 test instances per node; **a node below the floor is excluded from the IQM, kept in the PoI and reported.** Basis: D8-PASS (`mac-d8-pass-20261116` @ `d80cb043`, declared before running): the only candidate with per-arm false-pass ≤ 0.035 in every D8-SKEW scenario, on T1 × 12 and on T1 × 6; mean two-arm power 0.36 at g = 0.3 (μ ≥ 1), 0.26 on T1 × 6; its calibration comes from the positivity screen, which is disclosed. **作者补充：** T1 定为每节点 12 个实例；论文如实报告 gate 的功效；事后的 Johnson t（c″J）不采用。Gate v3 implements items 1–5, is independently reviewed, and is bound by R1-BX-D8-CONFIRM's SHA. The R1-BX-D8 analysis lock stands. |

| R1-S2C3-DIAG | **S2-C3（H200 上重放 B2 的 12 个 thinking-off root，jobs 759756–759758）按预声明标准记为 NOT PASS**（标准 1：ionosphere s9011 \|Δ\| 0.0683、credit s9011 0.0204、均值 0.0104；标准 3：25/38/25 对 20/33/20 ±10%），不放宽；基础设施项全部正常（12/12 rc 0、无 worker 崩溃、offload 61/61、parse 0/240、吞吐 < 2 × B2）。**追加一次预声明诊断：** 在同类 GPU 上把同样 12 个 root 再跑一遍，同一套指标比较两次 H200 运行。判定：若 H200 对 H200 的均值 \|Δ final_y\| ≥ H200 对 B2 均值的一半，或任一实例 \|Δ\| > 0.02，或三类比率任一相差超过 10 %，则认定差异来自采样不确定性，带披露继续启动；否则（H200 两次几乎一致却与 B2 不符）认定软件栈有差异，STOP 并排查。两次运行与结论都写入 sweep-2 报告。ENGINEERING_ONLY，无 B.2 审计，不触审计种子。 |
| R1-S2C3B-RERUN | **S2-C3b 在启动 commit 上重跑**（R1-S2C3B-LAUNCH）。此前在 `9ae6d877` 上 7/8 通过、`openml_wdbc` 未过 D28 的结果只作参考。若 wdbc 在启动 commit 上仍不过，报告未过的是 D28 的哪一条，由作者决定 7 个节点照常并披露，还是先排查。 |

| R1-TRACKP-SFT-DUP | **SFT 跨 split 重复提议：从 held-out（验证与测试）中排除与训练集重复的提议，作为主结果；同时报告含重复的完整 held-out 作为次要结果。** sweep-1 试跑时规模为训练 1,176 / 验证 47 / 测试 74（共 262 个有 label 的测试提议），sweep 2 按同一规则。 |

| R1-HARNESS-ISOLATION | **在任何 T1 label run 与任何 RL 训练之前，完成一个独立审查的 harness 隔离单元：** 可编辑代码在单独进程中运行；指标日志只可追加；超时用 cgroup 整体终止；出现多于一行指标的运行一律拒绝。依据：Track G 移植包的威胁模型（READY 第 56–58 项）——可编辑代码与冻结 wrapper 同进程，指标理论上可被伪造；RL 阶段模型可能学会利用这一点。在此之前，声明改动的审查与 S1 审计须逐个读 diff。 |

### 20 November 2026 (night) — sweep-2 C3b at the launch commit (author)

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-S2C3B-WDBC | At the launch commit `df58bcd5` the S2-C3b liveness was 7/8 (job 759810, H200; `ops-s2c3b-launch-stop-20261120`): openml_wdbc failed only D28 `invalid_rate` (observed 0.30, required < 0.3, i.e. 6 of 20 invalid); its active novelty (0.70) and keep after trial 1 (2) pass; the other seven nodes pass all criteria. H200 runs are deterministic, so a rerun at the same commit repeats the result. Decision (option A of R1-S2C3B-RERUN): sweep 2 submits all 16 chunks (8 nodes); the **primary sweep-2 analysis uses the seven nodes that passed C3b**, and openml_wdbc enters as a pre-declared sensitivity row (with / without), disclosed in the sweep-2 report and the appendix. The S2-C5 launch check accepts a sealed C3b record with 7/8 only if the single failed node is openml_wdbc and its only failed criterion is invalid_rate with requirement "< 0.3" and observed ≤ 0.30; everything else stays a STOP. This code change (`s2c3b-wdbc-exception-20261120`) is made by the coordinating chat, with tests and an independent review. The note's prose is updated by the cluster agent to cite this row and re-reviewed. C3b is rerun at the new launch commit and must reproduce exactly this result (7/8 with the same openml_wdbc failure) or give 8/8; otherwise STOP. For exactly this shortfall, this row amends the 8/8 requirement of R1-SWEEP2-AUTH (P1–P7 and the launch-commit rule), R1-S2C3B-LAUNCH, R1-S2C3B-RERUN and the sweep-2 note; every other liveness shortfall remains a STOP. |

| R1-TRACKG-AR-U2-TASL2 | Ada's GPUs are continuously occupied by other users; both attempts on an exclusive card were invaded by foreign processes within minutes and stopped under the no-shared-card rule (`ops-tasl-20261116` @ `ebe03cd5`, U4 addendum). The clean isolated smoke, the U2 unit and the slowdown measurement of autoresearch_fixedtokens (cond7a, kit `mac-trackg-iso-ar-cond7a-20261120` @ `7330338`, `frozen_pin_sha256` `147f0d35…`) move to TASL-2 on an exclusive RTX 4090 under the GPU lease. The harness-isolation host checks (READY 60 §3; `reports/HARNESS_ISOLATION_20261117.md` §3) run on TASL-2 first and must all pass; if any fails, STOP and return to waiting for an exclusive Ada window. TASL-7 is the fallback host for the same procedure. The census row names its host and discloses it as the one host difference in the census (T1 labels run on H200 under R1-T1-HOST-H200). Track P stays paused; the run takes the GPU lease and does not touch the attempt-1 archive. |
| R1-T1-HOST-TASL | The harness isolation does not work inside the HTCondor container on NEMO2 H200 (job 759828: no delegated cgroup v2 subtree, Yama ptrace_scope 0, and the parent is dumpable at the accept under apptainer, which is not waivable; `ops-t1-h200-isolation-20261121`, `reports/sweep2_20261116/T1_H200_ISOLATION_CHECK_20261121.md`). Ada, the declared fallback, is continuously occupied by other users (R1-TRACKG-AR-U2-TASL2). The T1 table (G.1b noise floors, G.4 pilot, G.5 labels) therefore runs on the two RTX 4090 hosts: the worker (training) runs on TASL-2 under the harness isolation; the proposer's llama-server (gemma4_31b, exam settings: context 32,768, `-ngl 999 -np 1 --jinja`, q8_0 KV, `cache_prompt` false, `{"enable_thinking":false}`) runs on TASL-7 and is reached over Tailscale. G.1b, which needs no proposer, may use the GPUs of both hosts. One GPU model (RTX 4090) for the whole table. Precondition: the harness-isolation host checks pass on TASL-2 (R1-TRACKG-AR-U2-TASL2), and on TASL-7 if it runs workers; otherwise STOP and return to the author. Track P's P.4 SFT training moves to NEMO2 H200 with image v2 (R1-QTORCH-AMEND), since SFT runs no agent-edited code. Amends the Ada fallback of R1-T1-HOST-H200. |

### 21 November 2026 (morning) — sweep-2 runtime errors, T1 rows on the TASL pair (author)

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-SWEEP2-RTE-DIAG | Sweep 2 (`s2ev-20261119-a`, launch commit `4aac5209`, clusters 759812–759827) stopped at the chunk worker gate: 6 worker `runtime_error`s (`exit code 1`, no metric) in four `append_only_summary` openml chunks (breast_w s145 t16, s155 t19; climate s264 t19, t20; credit_approval s200 t11; wdbc s150 t20); all other chunks and every `none` chunk have none; pooled failure rate 7.99 % (`ops-sweep2-launch-20261121`). H200 runs are deterministic, so a plain rerun would repeat the same proposals. Rule fixed before the diagnosis: the cluster agent replays each of the six trials twice (exact config and seed, same image and harness, CPU) and inspects stderr. (i) If all six crash deterministically with an exception raised by the proposed configuration values, they are proposal-induced failures, treated like `invalid_config`; the four chunks are accepted and the collection covers all 16 chunks, through a reviewed collection change that admits exactly these six diagnosed records; disclosed in the sweep-2 report. (ii) If any one does not reproduce or traces to infrastructure (OOM, I/O, signal, environment), the four chunks are rerun whole in new directories at a new launch commit (C3b first), and any `runtime_error` in the rerun is a STOP. Amends the "any runtime_error is a STOP" worker gate of the sweep-2 note for exactly these six records under (i). |
| R1-G1B-EDITS | G.1b measures each T1 node's **four** declared representative edits (E1–E4): (4 + 1) × 92 = **460 runs per node**, uniform across the T1 nodes (autoresearch_fixedtokens included when it joins). Amends the "552 runs per node" of R1-G1B-CAP, which assumed five edits; the D21 rule and the 100 GPU-h cap are unchanged. The screening funnel (R1-BX-D12) discloses that T1 nodes qualify out of four edits and T0 nodes out of five. Basis: `docs/r1/proposals/T1_TASL_ROWS_DRAFT_20261121.md` §1; the census U2 records' declared-edit manifests (four entries each; the spec's `edits_manifest_sha256`). |
| R1-G1B-TIMEOUT | For the T1 table (G.1b, G.4, G.5) the per-run timeout is **3 × the slowest of the node's B1, B2 and E1–E4 census records** (smoke records excluded; the U4 census on the RTX 6000 Ada, or the node's own census host where that is a TASL host, as for autoresearch_fixedtokens), the same rule for every T1 node. Amends R1-TRACKG-PORT-DECISIONS (4) ("3 × the census baseline") for the T1 table; T2 and later tables are unchanged. On the TASL hosts the census times are an estimate (except for a node censused on its TASL host): the first G.1b run that reaches the timeout is a STOP for the author, not a divergent row; a G.4 pilot whose run median exceeds the basis is a STOP; in G.4 and G.5 an edited proposal's timeout remains its failure, and a baseline timeout STOPs the campaign. Basis: `docs/r1/proposals/T1_TASL_ROWS_DRAFT_20261121.md` §2 (per-node values). |
| R1-T1-TASL-ENV | Hosts and environments of the T1 table on the TASL pair (R1-T1-HOST-TASL), in place of an image row. Hosts: TASL-2 (G.1b; G.4/G.5 workers; driver 535.309.01) and TASL-7 (G.1b; proposer server; driver 580.178.04), each identified by host name and the full UUID of its RTX 4090; a driver change during the table is a STOP. The G.1b nodes measured on TASL-7 also run their default parts on TASL-2, and the metrics must be identical, else STOP and that node's G.1b is re-measured on TASL-2. Node environments built with uv from the census freezes (torch312, jax312; Python 3.12.16), equal to the freeze at every job start, else STOP; uv version and interpreter source recorded. Harness at the commit the signed note names, clean. The harness-isolation host checks (READY 60 §3) passed on every host that runs workers, in a report on main bound by sha256. Each job non-root, Yama ptrace_scope ≥ 1, in a delegated user scope (MemoryMax 8G, MemorySwapMax 0) as the census, holding the host's GPU lease (`~/r1_locks/gpu0.lock`) with no other compute process on the card. Proposer: the Track X exam server on TASL-7 used in place — executable sha256 `b70f4882…7d8b` (`version: 0.7.0-dev (build 1, commit 620d222)`) and its whole build directory bound by a sha256 manifest, llama.cpp libraries resolved inside it, no LD_LIBRARY_PATH/LD_PRELOAD — with the pinned gemma4_31b GGUF, the exam settings, on TASL-7's Tailscale address. Node data from each node's setup command, manifest equal to the census. Basis: `docs/r1/proposals/T1_TASL_ROWS_DRAFT_20261121.md` §3; `sweep2/t1_tasl/T1_TASL_PLAN.md`. |

### 22 November 2026 (d) — submission version, arms-freeze cut-off, closed loop, cuts, lighter process, C1 position (author)

The author adopted the recommendations of the chat-session review of 22 Nov 2026 (not quoted; recorded by the chat-session Claude, AI-assisted; the recommendations are filed as `proposals/MINSUB_RECOMMENDATIONS_20261122.md`). No signed note, preregistration or code guard is changed by this section, and R1-G5-DECISIONS and R1-SWEEP2-PREREG stand as they are. The rows were revised once after the independent review `reviews/MINSUB_PLAN_REVIEW_20261122.md`. A two-page summary of the plan as it now stands is `docs/r1/CURRENT.md` (R1-CURRENT-DOC).

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-SUBMISSION-MIN | **The submission version of Paper B** consists of four parts, and work that serves none of them waits. Target: ICML 2027, abstract 14 Jan and full paper 22 Jan 2027, anywhere on earth (`icml.cc/Conferences/2027/Dates`, read 22 Nov 2026; R1-MINSUB-CHOICES (2)). These dates replace the projection in the RUNBOOK's venue note (about 16 Jan and 22 Jan). (1) **C1**: control errors measured from the fresh-seed audits of sweep 1 and sweep 2 on T0 (two proposers). (2) **C2**: the theory. The main text carries only statements whose author proof check is recorded. On 22 Nov these are T4 Propositions 8′, 10(a′) and 10(c) with the errata of R1-T4-PROOF-CHECK. Every other statement (the T2 propositions, T4 Propositions 6, 7 and 9, T4 10(b)) goes to the appendix marked "not author-checked", or is dropped, unless a row records its check by 3 Jan 2027. (3) **C3 offline**: the preregistered gate (R1-SWEEP2-PREREG, unchanged), read once, on the T0 node set of PRE-WDBC and on the T1 node set of the arms-frozen record (R1-ARMS-FREEZE-CUTOFF). (4) **Closed loop on T0** (R1-D3-MIN). Anything else is optional: it enters the submission only if it is finished and reviewed by 3 Jan 2027 without delaying (1)–(4). **Both outcomes are prepared.** The gate's simulated power is low (D8-PASS as R1-BX-D8-V3-B quotes it, with its qualifiers: mean two-arm power 0.36 at effect size 0.3; 0.26 for T1 × 6). If the gate does not pass, RUNBOOK B.7 and §6 apply as written (C3 is reported as the measured negative with T4 Proposition 7's dispersion bound; C1 and C2 carry the paper). Proposition 7 is among the statements not yet author-checked, so its check comes first, before the gate read (R1-MINSUB-CHOICES (3)). This row adds: effect sizes with intervals for every arm, and the closed-loop result of the rule arms (D.3a). **Amends PLAN §9** for the submission version: item 2 (baselines) is reduced as R1-D3-MIN states; of item 4 (ablations), the ladder itself and the preregistered secondary arms stay, and the action, information and model-size ablations are optional under the 3 Jan rule; item 5's second manager in closed loop is cut (the second proposer stays in C1 and in the separate descriptive table the preregistration allows), and its held-out-suite transfer is reported as a STOP if R1-ARMS-FREEZE-CUTOFF (d) applies; item 6 holds for main-text statements only. Each reduction is listed in the manuscript's deviations table. |
| R1-ARMS-FREEZE-CUTOFF | **The arms-frozen record is committed no later than 20 Dec 2026**, and the single primary gate read follows it. The schedule's own targets are earlier (R1-G5-DECISIONS: gate read about 15 Dec at K = 10, which the date model places three days after the T1 labels are complete; about 11 Dec at K = 6 after a published K-check row). The cut-off applies only if the T1 table slips. Rules at the cut-off: (a) **Release date.** The T1 train/dev release row (R1-T1-LABELS-UNSEAL) is written no later than the cut-off minus the time the arms need to be trained, selected and calibrated (preregistration §4 step 3, including fold and secondary arms). Until that time is measured on the T0 arms, the planning value is three days, borrowed from the date model's interval between complete labels and the gate read (`analysis_days_to_b7`), so the release is on 17 Dec at the latest. The measurement is made early: phase-1 SFT alone is projected at 14–19 GPU-h (R1-TRACKP-P4-DECISIONS). (b) **Which nodes.** The release row names its nodes. PRE-T1 A applies unchanged: a node with at least one labelled train and one labelled dev instance is supplied, and the preregistered test floor decides whether it enters the IQM. A node below that is left out of the T1 node set. (c) **A left-out node, or an instance of a supplied node that is not in the arms-frozen record.** Its label runs may finish, but its label, test and evaluation records stay sealed: PRE-T1 A allows no later read with more T1 nodes. Only label-free facts are reported (run counts, run status, the noise floor). A read needs a signed preregistration amendment. (d) **Leave-one-suite-out.** If any suite has no labelled T1 node in the frozen set, the leave-one-suite-out analysis STOPs as a whole and is reported as a STOP (R1-BX-D8-CONFIRM-V31); the aggregate gate and the other secondary analyses still run. `autoresearch_fixedtokens` is the only node of its suite. If it misses the release, the suite-level generalisation test is lost, which is the cost R1-G5-DECISIONS (G5-AR A) accepted 138 GPU-h to avoid. This row accepts that loss only at the cut-off, in exchange for a gate read that leaves time for the paper. (e) The cut-off moves only by an author row written before it is reached. |
| R1-D3-MIN | **Closed-loop evaluation for the submission is on T0 only**, with the main proposer (`gemma4_31b`, thinking off). Amends RUNBOOK D.2 and D.3 ("T0, T1 and T2 nodes", two repetitions), replaces the online pilot C.3, and exempts stage D.3a from the "only if B.7 passes" condition of Phases C and D. **One pre-declaration covers both stages** and is committed with its hash before any D.3 run starts (RUNBOOK §1). It fixes: the T0 node set (the PRE-WDBC primary set unless it says otherwise); the memory mode or modes; the run budget W and the LLM-call cap, equal for all arms; 18 deployment instances per node with their ids, disjoint from every sweep and split-file instance; seeds; K, the seeds and the host of the fresh-seed audit that gives Y; every contrast of both stages (each rule arm against A0; A3 and A5 against A2 and against A0; and A6 against A5, should A6 enter), sample sizes, multiplicity, and the analysis script. Each stage also needs its authorization note. **D.3a (rule arms; not conditional on the gate):** A0 (the Karpathy default of R1-BX-D7), A1, A2, the fixed-r replication + test baseline of R1-BX-D7, and one anytime-valid commit gate (the PACE-style gate of §5's required list, an e-process on replicate runs, charged for its runs). A1's constants are those fitted under PRE-A2 C on the dev label block; they are pinned by sha256 in the pre-declaration and enter the arms-frozen record unchanged. D.3a therefore starts only after the sweep-2 train/dev release (which waits for the registered sweep-2 audit, R1-SWEEP2-RELEASE-DECISIONS). It reads no test label, no evaluation value and no T1 label, and its outputs are not used to train, tune or select any arm. It gives C2 its closed-loop evidence (index policy against the default at matched compute) and exercises the deployment path early. **D.3b (learned arms; only if the gate passes):** A3 and A5 as frozen in the arms-frozen record, on D.3a's instances and seeds, so that arms are paired. A6 enters only if it is trained under RUNBOOK P.5 after the gate passes, on the train and dev label blocks only, and frozen in its own record before D.3b starts; it is optional under R1-SUBMISSION-MIN's 3 Jan rule. If the gate does not pass, D.3b is not run. One repetition is required; a second is optional. **Baselines.** For the submission version the closed-loop comparison set is the five D.3a arms. The other entries of §5's required list (a separate noise-aware keep threshold, racing-style rule, harness heuristics, random at matched realised budget, the AWR-MLP controller) and the reference arms of R1-C3 (ladder) (SAGE-style gate, FOREAGENT-style pairwise predictor, Ladder margin rule, posterior-mean incumbent) are not run in closed loop. None is added to an offline test table by this row: an arm reads test only if it is in the arms-frozen record and inside the preregistration's PRE-SECONDARY scope. **Hosts.** Proposer calls run on an HTCondor GPU model with a measured chunk time, as in sweep 2, so that D.3 takes no TASL card from the T1 table. The audit host is named in the pre-declaration; deepthought was allowed for the sweep-2 audit only (R1-SWEEP2-B2-GO), so using it again needs a row. |
| R1-SUBMISSION-CUTS | **Cut from the submission version** (each may return in a later version; none is dropped from the record): tier **T2** and the transfer to T2 nodes (D.1's transfer part, G.6); the **API proposer** (R1-BX-D6); closed-loop runs on **T1** nodes; arm **R-B** (optional since R1-BX-D9); the **aggregation round** C.2 (R1-BX-D11); the **debiased-memory A/B** of C.4; the **information-matched TabPFN comparison** (R1-BX-D13; the native TabPFN row stays); closed-loop evaluation of **non-qualifying nodes** (R1-BX-D12; the screening funnel per suite is still reported). **C4** in the submission is the decision-independence condition stated as an assumption, with the offline checks that R1-T4-PROOF-CHECK placed in T4 §6; the forced-branch test stays deferred, as that row decided, and belongs to the follow-up paper. **A4 and A6.** The three-way comparison keeps A4 (self-control) against A5 offline, as a preregistered secondary arm. A6 has no offline test read: it is trained after the gate (RUNBOOK P.5), so it is not in the arms-frozen record, and a test read would need a signed amendment. It can appear only in D.3b. A hybrid arm is reported only if it is frozen with the other arms. **Secondary analyses** stay as preregistered. Fold arms are trained before the arms-frozen record (preregistration §4 step 3). A fold analysis whose arms are not all in that record is not run, then or later, and the omission is listed with the deviations. The multi-step RL design task of `prompts/FOLLOWUP_PROMPTS_20261119.md` item 3 is not on Paper B's path and takes no time from (1)–(4) of R1-SUBMISSION-MIN. |
| R1-PROCESS-TIERS | **Review and record-keeping by tier, from this row on.** **Tier E (evidence)** keeps the present procedure unchanged (note or pre-declaration, a committed independent review re-checked until it writes "accepted", hash bindings, STOP rules). It covers: evidence runs; anything that reads a sealed or test partition; preregistrations, pre-declarations and authorization notes; launch bindings; gate and fold-analysis code; code that writes, admits or parses label or evaluation records (audit, collection, label-run drivers, metric parsers, release checks); arm training, selection and calibration code; the rule layer, the deployment controller and the noise-floor code, which compute every keep and execute decision and δ_n, σ_node and g; the arms-frozen record; and the analysis scripts of D.3. A draft takes the tier of the unit it becomes, so a draft note, a preregistration draft and a launch prompt are tier E. **Tier S (support)**: other code and documents that a tier-E unit depends on get RUNBOOK §1's rule as written: one fresh-session review, committed under `reviews/`, and one correction round. Remaining minor findings are fixed without a further round; a second round happens only after a MAJOR finding. **Tier D (drafts)**: manuscript text, proposals that are not drafts of a tier-E unit, `CURRENT.md` and ENGINEERING dry runs get no separate review record; the coordinating chat checks them at merge, and the manuscript gets its mock review before submission. A unit's record is its commits, its test output and one dated STATUS line; a separate report file is written only for tier E and for a STOP. The coordinating chat may put several recommended options into one proposed author row per day; the author decides the row, and work that depends on it waits for it, as before. When unsure of a unit's tier, the higher tier applies. This row changes no code guard and no signed note. |
| R1-CURRENT-DOC | **`docs/r1/CURRENT.md` is the short statement of the plan as it stands**: claims, arms, data, gates, hosts, dates, what is frozen and what is open, in at most two pages. It is a view, not an authority: PLAN §10 rows control wherever they differ, each line cites its row or record, and it introduces no decision and no state that is not recorded elsewhere. A date without a row is marked "target". The coordinating chat updates it in the same merge as any author row that changes it. STATUS.md stays the progress log; its first line points to CURRENT.md. Amends RUNBOOK §1 ("PLAN.md, this runbook and STATUS.md are the only planning documents") by adding this view. |
| R1-C1-POSITION | **C1 is not claimed as a first measurement, and the novelty sweep is extended** (`inputs/NOVELTY_SWEEP_PART3_20261122.md`; amends C3_DESIGN §10; the rerun within four weeks of submission, PLAN §9 item 8, still stands). Two NeurIPS 2026 workshop papers, found after the 18–19 Oct sweep, measure noise-driven false acceptance in autonomous experimentation. The statistical-gate family, which already held PACE (9 Oct sweep) and SAGE (18 Oct sweep), has further members (SGM and its descendants, an evidence-governed adoption framework). C1 is therefore positioned as supporting evidence under governance: fresh-seed audited ground truth on the T0 nodes, two proposers, duplicate and inert edits counted as wasted runs. The paper's stated differences are: (a) the rule maximises audited gain per run under a declared run price, where the gate family controls a false-accept probability; (b) screening before a run is spent, from a per-proposal forecast of the increment distribution; (c) the theory for a ledger-dependent proposer; (d) a learned forecast against a theory-derived index policy at matched compute. C3_DESIGN §10's statement that no prior work does decision-focused post-training of a predictor feeding an accept/skip rule is narrowed: "Calibration Is Not Control" (arXiv 2606.21399; in the 9 Oct sweep) makes intervention advantage, the expected utility gain from intervening, the decision object in place of a calibrated risk score, and is cited as the closest precedent (read at abstract level on 22 Nov; the full text is read before the manuscript states how it trains). The paper claims only the specific combination: a forecast of an audited increment distribution feeding a closed-form keep/skip rule under a run price. The DAC statement is made precise: the learned object is a forecaster and the policy is a derived rule applied to it; the reduction to one-step decisions is a conditional theorem with synthetic controls, not an empirical finding; the offline one-step utility is a proxy for closed-loop value that relies on decision independence. One anytime-valid gate is a reference arm in D.3a (R1-D3-MIN), as the 9 Oct sweep already required for PACE. The term "RL" is not used in the submission version: A6 is described as decision-focused fine-tuning, and if P.5's GRPO check is reported, its gradient estimator is named. Statements about external papers in this row carry the verification level given in the novelty note; figures are checked against the PDFs before the manuscript quotes them. |
| R1-MINSUB-CHOICES | **The author's choices on the points this section left open** (22 Nov, after reading them; not quoted). (1) **Cut-off.** R1-ARMS-FREEZE-CUTOFF stands with its rule (d): the loss of the leave-one-suite-out analysis is accepted at the cut-off. To make that unlikely, the G.5 label runs, which have one worker slot on the TASL pair (R1-T1-HOST-TASL), take this order: the cheapest node of each of the other two suites; then `autoresearch_fixedtokens`, as soon as its noise floor (G.1b) is sealed; then the remaining nodes, cheapest first. Every suite then has a labelled node as early as one slot allows. The drafter of the G.5 note writes this order into the note before it is signed; this row does not change that note or the host rule. The coordinating chat reports what the K-check of R1-BX-D3 would cost; K = 6 still needs its own published row. (2) **ICML 2027.** The official pages were read on 22 Nov: abstract 14 Jan 2027 and full paper 22 Jan 2027, both anywhere on earth; first-stage rejection notices 23 Feb; final decisions 3 May (`icml.cc/Conferences/2027/Dates`). Quotas (`icml.cc/Conferences/2027/ReciprocalReviewingAndSubmissionQuotas`): each submission names an eligible reciprocal reviewer at abstract registration, who reviews from 24 Feb to 16 Mar; an author is eligible with one paper accepted by 14 Jan at a listed venue (workshop papers do not count); a submission with no eligible author is exempt, but each of its authors may have only one such submission. Each submission also selects one contribution type. The author settles the author list, the reciprocal reviewer and the contribution type before 14 Jan. (3) **Proof check.** Order: T4 Proposition 7 first, because the negative-outcome text rests on it, together with what it builds on (T2 Proposition 1 and T4 Proposition 6, the keep rule); then T4 Proposition 9 (the winner's curse under an adaptive proposer); then the other T2 statements the main text uses. T4 10(b) goes to the appendix as not author-checked, or is dropped. The first two are checked before the gate read (target: about 10 Dec), so that the choice of main-text statements does not depend on the gate's outcome. The coordinating chat prepares one packet per statement (statement, proof, audit findings, points to confirm); those for Propositions 7 and 9 are on branch `mac-proofpackets-20261122`. (4) **D.3.** The coordinating chat drafts the single pre-declaration of R1-D3-MIN as a tier-E unit, with these values unless the draft's review finds a reason against one: the PRE-WDBC primary T0 node set; memory mode `append_only_summary`; W = 20, as in sweep 2; 18 instances per node; K = 10 for the audit of Y. The LLM-call cap has no value on `main` (sweep 2 had no skips): the draft proposes one, equal for all arms, and the author fixes it by signing. Audit host: deepthought CPUs, which this row allows for the D.3 audit under the conditions of R1-SWEEP2-B2-GO (all D.3 audits on this one host, the environment basis, the harness-code equality check, the host difference disclosed). **One arm is added to D.3a, which amends R1-D3-MIN to six D.3a arms:** the Ladder margin rule, keep iff Δ̂ > η, with one η per node found by direct search on the dev label block. It has no screen: with a constant prior a screen makes the same decision for every proposal. This moves the Ladder margin rule from R1-D3-MIN's list of reference arms not run in closed loop into D.3a. The search objective (computed from the dev label block only, with no evaluation value), the search procedure and the resulting constants are pinned by sha256 in the pre-declaration. The arm reads the same data as A1 and no test label. Its contrasts (against A0 and against A2) are in the pre-declaration. An offline test read of this arm needs a signed preregistration amendment. |
| R1-THEORY-RHO | **How the theory treats the correlation ϱ of CRN errors on CPU nodes** (author, 22 Nov, after reading the analysis; not quoted). Background: T4 Proposition 9 and T2 Proposition 4(a) assume that, given the history, a candidate's error depends on it only through the incumbent's error (ε \| e ∼ N(ϱe, σ_c²)). A check on the PILOT E1 runs of 22 Oct (`theory/author_packets_20261122/check_kernel_e1.py`, AI analysis, unreviewed) finds this screening property roughly met on the OpenML nodes and not met on `digits_mlp` (partial correlation of sibling edits given the default +0.37, bootstrap interval +0.24 to +0.51), which points to a component shared by the configurations of a campaign. Decisions: (1) **T4 Proposition 9 is restated**: the main statement is the kernel-free inequality (on every keep, for every proposer and every error law, the new record error exceeds the old one minus the true increment; for a null proposal it rises on every keep); the truncated-normal formula becomes a corollary under the kernel, with its scope stated. (2) **T4 Proposition 6 and arms A1/A2 are not changed**; the text states that the rule is Bayes-optimal under (A2), and that with a selected incumbent under CRN the Bayes threshold moves down by (1 − ϱ_n)E[e | F_n]; under the conditions stated in T4 §2 (Gaussian approximation, E[e | F_n] ≥ 0, Var(e | F_n) ≤ σ², μ_n ≤ 0) the deployed rule is then conservative (it keeps too few, not too many); for μ_n > 0 the direction is not determined. The size on `digits_mlp` is reported as a limitation. No preregistered constant or D.3 arm changes. (3) **T2 Proposition 4(a) gets the same scope statement**; the `digits_mlp` example in T2 §5 is given as a range over the two error models (0.53 to 0.63) or removed. (4) **T5's feedback simulation is re-run with a shared-component error model** at `digits_mlp`-like parameters, ENGINEERING_ONLY, to see whether its conclusions change; the result is an input to the manuscript, not evidence. (5) **Before the check above enters the paper**, the author accepts the 22 Oct E1 PILOT data (still open) and a direct ancestor–descendant test replaces the sibling proxy; both are open. (6) The same mechanism motivates Paper C's re-audit-the-incumbent channel and may be cited there. The restated statements still need the author's proof check before the main text uses them (R1-MINSUB-CHOICES (3)). |
| R1-D3-CHOICES | **The author's choices on §13 of the D.3 pre-declaration draft and on the threshold correction** (author, 22 Nov, after reading the recommendations; not quoted). (1) **Threshold correction for a selected incumbent: settle by a direct test first.** The ENGINEERING_ONLY T5 re-run with a shared-component error model (`docs/r1/theory/T5_SHARED_NOISE_20261122.md`, branch `mac-t5-shared-20261122`) sizes the gain from correcting A2's threshold on `digits_mlp` at +0.47 δ_n (feasible, keep-count rule) to +1.84 δ_n (oracle) per campaign, and finds A1 below the default rule there; its calibration rests on PILOT data and does not reproduce the E1 pattern. Before any arm changes, a direct ancestor–descendant test of the screening property is run on data that may be read under the current rows (scope to be established first; no sealed partition). If the test supports a shared component on a node, a seventh D.3a arm "A2 with a keep-count threshold correction" is proposed before the D.3 pre-declaration is signed; otherwise R1-THEORY-RHO (2) stands. A1/A2 and the preregistration are not changed either way. (2) **§13 of `D3_PREDECLARATION_DRAFT_20261122.md`:** every item is settled at the draft's recommended value: LLM-call cap 80 (= 4W); two-sided tests, Holm within each stage, α = 0.05; one repetition; `PaceStyleGate` as on main; Welch for FIXR3; `openml_wdbc` not run; the LADDER search and fit population as written; no further row for the sweep-2 release; Y divided by σ_node; a 140 GPU-h projection STOP; the verify-seed check outside the pinned script; D.3b pins identical to D.3a's; the two pre-admitted runtime-error classes and the 12.99 % failure STOP with replicate failures counted; the replicate mean as the memory line; D.4 analyses not in this pre-declaration; each missing implementation as a reviewed tier-E unit; the failure rules and seed blocks as written. Item 16 (the D.3b sidecar image) stays open until its three missing facts are known; D.3b cannot run before the gate passes. If (1) adds an arm, §13 gains an item and the seed blocks and the analysis script change; signing waits for (1). |
| R1-E1-PILOT-ACCEPT | **The 22 Oct E1 noise-floor runs are accepted** (author, 23 Nov; not quoted): `results/r1/pilot/E1_20261022T214606Z/` and its report `reports/NOISE_FLOOR_COMMON_20261022.md` stop being PILOT pending acceptance and may be cited as measured noise floors and per-edit correlations, with the limits their report states (30 seeds; one edit away from the default configuration). This satisfies the first half of R1-THEORY-RHO (5); the sibling check on these runs (`theory/author_packets_20261122/check_kernel_e1.py`) stays an unreviewed proxy and is not a result until the direct measurement below. |
| R1-SCREEN-MEASURE | **A direct measurement of the screening property is run before the D.3 pre-declaration is signed** (author, 23 Nov, choosing the recommendation of `reports/SCREENING_TEST_20261122.md` §4.2 on branch `mac-screening-test-20261122`; not quoted). Design as recommended there: on all eight T0 nodes, the default configuration, its five E1 single edits and two-edit children c0 + e_i + e_j, each on 120 fresh joint seeds; the test of that report (pooled partial correlation of child and grandparent given the parent, bootstrap interval, SHARED above 0.1, NO_SHARED below 0.1), with the corrected power of its §4.2 (0.95 at p = 0.24; 0.82 for NO_SHARED under the kernel). It is **tier E**, so that it can enter the paper: a pre-declaration with its seed block (checked against every reserved namespace), code, analysis script, STOP rules and an authorization note, reviewed until accepted, then signed by the author. | R1-PROOF-POLICY | **Which theory statements may enter the main text** (author, 23 Nov, after being told the recorder recommended keeping the author's own check; not quoted). **Amends R1-SUBMISSION-MIN (2) and R1-MINSUB-CHOICES (3).** A statement may enter the main text when (a) the independent AI proof check (`theory/author_packets_20261122/AI_PROOF_CHECK_20261123.md` and its successors) rates it "ready" as written or after its proposed changes, those changes are applied, and nothing in it is rated a gap or false; and (b) the coordinating chat has spot-checked it independently: it re-derives at least the statement's key identity or inequality and one edge case, records what it checked and the result in the same folder, and finds no disagreement with (a). The author's own check is no longer required; statements the author has already checked (T4 8′, 10(a′), 10(c), R1-T4-PROOF-CHECK) keep that status. The theory notes and the manuscript mark each statement's status precisely ("author-checked" or "AI-checked under R1-PROOF-POLICY"), and no text says or implies that the author checked a statement he did not. The author remains responsible for every statement in the paper. A statement rated a gap or false by either check stays out of the main text until it is fixed and both checks pass again. |

The manuscript's figures from these papers are checked against their PDFs before submission. Tier D. |
| R1-CONTROLS-ROW | **One positive and one negative control per node, reported in the appendix** (author, 23 Nov; not quoted; after Skill2Env's Oracle/NOP gate, done with noise in view). *Positive control:* for each T0 node, the probability that the K = 10 fresh-seed audit, read with the paper's audit statistic, detects each E1 single edit whose effect is known from the accepted 22 Oct runs (R1-E1-PILOT-ACCEPT), computed by resampling 10 of the 30 joint seeds; it reads only those accepted runs and can be done now (tier S). *Negative control:* for each node and rule (the default, A1, A2), the share of no-op proposals kept. On bit-exact CPU nodes a strict rule keeps no no-op by construction, which the table states; the informative cells are nodes whose runs are not bit-exact. Neither control is a test or changes any gate, and neither reads a test label or evaluation value. Optional under R1-SUBMISSION-MIN's 3 Jan rule, except that the positive control is done first because it costs no runs and supports the reading of a negative gate result. |

Only `digits_mlp` and `openml_climate` are testable; the other six T0 nodes are NOT_TESTED by design and their runs are descriptive; the all-triples reading is reported as secondary. ### 26 November 2026 (b) — TASL-2 CPUs 8-11 (author)

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-TASL2-CPU | TASL2-CPU A. In the MetaMaze determinism stress on TASL-2 (`reports/tasl_chain_20261122/TASL2_CPU_STABILITY_20261125.md`, review ACCEPTED), CPUs 8-11 (physical cores 4 and 5) failed 3 of 3 runs, while CPUs 0-3, 4-7 and 12-15 passed 3 of 3 each with bit-identical outputs (microcode 0x133). From now on no R1 job on TASL-2 uses CPUs 8-11. A spec or job that pins a CPU set containing any of them gets a replacement set of the same size that avoids 8-11 (the Mac proposes it in the next spec or note; the coordinating chat publishes it with that unit), and that set passes a fixed-seed determinism check before its first use. Sealed results that used CPUs 8-15 stand: their fixed-seed determinism passed, and for `mlgym_houseprice` the xc3 comparison against TASL-7 was bit-identical. |

The preregistered offline gate (R1-SWEEP2-PREREG, gate v3.1) is unchanged and is read once as preregistered; in the paper it is the confirmatory test of the one-step claim (a learned forecast improves the one-step decisions on logged proposals against A2), and it no longer decides which closed-loop arms run. The gate's Holm pair and D.3's contrast families answer different questions, each keeps its declared error control, and both are reported whatever they show. The outcome table of the proposal's §6 is the declared reading. R1-SUBMISSION-MIN's four parts stay; `canon/PAPER_B_PLAN.md` and the manuscript's contribution list follow this row, and the change is listed in the deviations table. |

The D.3b family gains the contrast A5 − A3. A6 stays as R1-D3-MIN states. With A0, A1 and A2 it forms the 2 × 2 of rule layer × index policy. Contrasts added to the D.3a family: A0R − A0 and A2 − A0R. The arm reads no label and gets no offline test read by this row. |
| R1-D3-OUTCOMES2 | **Secondary outcomes of D.3, descriptive and without a verdict, written into the pre-declaration before any run:** (a) the budget curve Y(w) for w = 5, 10, 15, 20: the incumbent after the w-th charged worker run, audited as Y is; (b) compute per campaign: worker runs and worker seconds, LLM calls and tokens, proposer GPU-seconds, controller inference seconds, wall-clock time; (c) runs-to-match: per node, the smallest w at which an arm's mean Y(w) reaches A0's mean Y(20); (d) mechanism counts per campaign: duplicates proposed and executed, rule skips, value skips, keeps, distinct configurations executed, the last charged run that improved the audited incumbent; (e) offline–online agreement: per node and arm, the gate's per-node utility beside the arm's closed-loop Y, with the rank agreement of the arms, from outputs the preregistered analysis already writes; (f) A5 − A3 and A5 − A2 by tier from the per-node and per-tier tables of preregistration §7 (a read beyond those tables needs a signed amendment before the arms-frozen record). The unit states each node's configuration space and its review checks it against the edits the proposal schema admits. Descriptive, outside the Holm families; written into the D.3 pre-declaration. The result is reported whichever way it falls. |

### 26 November 2026 (e) — agents work from the repository; who decides what; which model runs which role (author)

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-AUTONOMY | **How the agents work together without the author carrying messages, and who decides what.** (1) **Messages.** Tasks and reports move through the repository as `canon/README.md` "How work moves" describes: the coordinator writes one file per task to branch `coord/coordinator`; each agent reports on its own branch `coord/<role>`; one writer per branch; these branches carry messages only. An agent picks up its tasks without waiting for the author. (2) **Decision levels** as in `canon/README.md` "Who decides what". Level 0: the agent doing the work settles execution and engineering questions itself and states them in its report. Level 1: the coordinator settles routing, priorities, the publication of a note that a row pre-authorises, and engineering disagreements between agents. Level 2 stays with the author: claims and scope; signatures of preregistrations, pre-declarations and evidence authorization notes; any read of a sealed or test partition; any change to a signed file; a cap raised, or a new host or GPU model for a table; a milestone that slips; a STOP that concerns the validity of evidence; deletion; credentials; anything PLAN does not cover. When unsure, the higher level applies. (3) **Publishing `main` without a per-push instruction.** The coordinator may merge to `main` when all of these hold: the unit is reviewed and accepted as its tier requires (R1-PROCESS-TIERS); it is documentation only, or the full `r1/tests` suite shows zero new failures against its base; PLAN §10 gains only rows the author has signed or rows under (4); the D8 guard passes; the merge is a merge commit; and it neither sets `approved: true` on a note, nor signs or changes a preregistration or pre-declaration, nor changes a signed file, unless a row pre-authorises exactly that publication. Any other push to `main` still waits for the author's "推 main". (4) **Coordinator rows.** Where the drafter of a unit recommends an option, an independent review has accepted the unit, and the choice is not level 2, the coordinator adopts the recommendation and records it in PLAN §10 as a row marked "(coordinating chat, under R1-AUTONOMY)". The author may overturn such a row at any time; work that rested on it is redone. (5) **What the author is told.** One digest a day, in Chinese: what was merged, which coordinator rows were written, STOPs, movement of dates, and what waits for the author, each item with a recommendation and what it blocks. A level-2 item that blocks work is sent at once. (6) **Unchanged by this row:** no agent pushes `main`; no force push; nothing is deleted; no credential is read; sealed data stays sealed until its release row; an evidence run starts only from the launch commit a signed note names; the rules 1 to 9 of `canon/README.md`. (7) The author may withdraw (3) or (4) by one sentence; the coordinator then returns to asking before every push. |
| R1-AGENT-RUNTIMES | **Which model runs which role** (author, 26 Nov 2026): the experiment agent and the cluster agent run on DeepSeek; the writing and brainstorming agent runs on Claude Fable 5.2 (flagship), soft limit only; the coordinator runs on Claude Opus 5.5, soft limit only, and is kept running in a tmux session where it watches the report branches and hands out tasks; the local worker pushes its own branches to GitHub itself (never `main`), so its units no longer need bundles. Consequences recorded by the coordinating chat: the writing and brainstorming role gets its own `SOUL.md` (`canon/agents/writer/`), beside the coordinator's; and **one coordinator at a time**: the file `COORDINATOR` on branch `coord/coordinator` names the session that holds the role, only that session publishes `main` and writes tasks, and a hand-over is one commit to that file by the session taking over. Until the tmux coordinator takes over, this chat session holds the role. |

### 26 November 2026 (f) — one compute agent; experiments not tied to a card (author)

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-AGENT-COMPUTE | **The cluster agent and the experiment agent become one compute agent** (author, 26 Nov 2026; amends R1-AGENT-RUNTIMES and the role list of R1-CANON-FOLDER). It runs on DeepSeek, reaches every host by SSH (deepthought and the ETP pool, TASL-2, TASL-7, Ada), finds which machine has free resources and runs the work there. Its `SOUL.md` is `canon/agents/compute/`; its tasks are under `tasks/compute/` on `coord/coordinator` and its reports on `coord/compute`. The open tasks of the two former roles pass to it. |
| R1-HOST-FREE | **Experiments are not tied to a host or a GPU model unless the measurement needs it** (author, 26 Nov 2026: "我们实验里能不和卡绑定就不和卡绑定"). (1) A job runs on any host and card that fits it and passes its environment check; the compute agent chooses; every job record names the host, the GPU model, the driver and the environment freeze. For work not yet under a signed note this replaces the rule of one host type or one GPU model per table (24 Oct; R1-HTCONDOR; the T1 host rows; the H200 of the P.4 draft; "one GPU model for both stages" of the D.3 draft). (2) What stays bound is the coordinating chat's reading of "unless the measurement needs it", and the author may strike any item: (a) runs that are differenced or compared bit for bit stay on one hardware class: a fixed-seed determinism check, the candidate and incumbent audits of one proposal, the start and final audits of one campaign's Y; (b) a node's results from a second host are used with the first host's noise floor only when that node has a cross-host PASS between the two (the default part bit-identical, as in xc2 and xc3); otherwise its floor is measured on the second host first; (c) in a paired closed-loop design all arms of one instance are served by one GPU model, and a campaign keeps its proposer on one GPU model from start to end. (3) A run under a signed note launches as that note says; moving it needs a re-issued note. Of the notes not yet signed, the G.5 note is signed as drafted for the TASL pair so that it can start, and further hosts join it through an addendum note; the P.4 phase-1 note and the D.3 pre-declaration are revised to this row before signature. (4) The paper states per table which hardware produced which rows; an analysis by hardware class beyond the preregistered ones needs a signed amendment before the arms-frozen record. (5) GPU-hour caps count GPU-hours on any card. |

### 26 November 2026 (g) — shared cards; no wall-clock limit and no calendar date decides; work proceeds in the order of its steps (author)

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| R1-CARD-SHARE | **A job may run on a card that another process is using** (author, 26 Nov 2026: "速度显存不对实验结果构成影响，可靠性我自己负责"). (1) A job is placed on any card that has the free memory it needs, whether or not another user's process or another of our jobs is on that card. The compute agent takes an empty card when one exists and shares when none does; it never touches another user's process. (2) Speed is not a result. The wall time, throughput and GPU-hours of an attempt on a shared card are recorded and are descriptive only. (3) The author accepts the reliability risk of sharing. An attempt lost because of the card or the host (out of memory beside another process, a kill, a reset, a lost connection) is an infrastructure failure: it is sealed and recorded with the card's other compute processes and their memory, and the same run is attempted again in a new attempt directory. It is never an outcome and never a STOP, and the number of attempts is not limited. (4) Every job record names the other compute processes on its card at the start and at the end (count and memory only). (5) R1-HOST-FREE (2)(a) to (c) are unchanged: a shared card is the same hardware class as an empty one. Launch code that is already reviewed (expansion G.1b at `7d0279d8`; G.5 revision 13) keeps its lease check, which refuses to start beside another user's compute process, until a reviewed unit changes it; that change is engineering, not a decision. New launchers (P.4 and D.3 outside HTCondor, further audit hosts) check free memory, not an empty card. |
| R1-NO-TIMEOUT | **No wall-clock limit decides a check or an outcome** (author, 26 Nov 2026: "我们把超时的所有限制…全部抹除"). The row applies to every run not yet launched; a run under a signed note follows its note. (1) **Withdrawn:** the wall-time bounds in the pass criteria of checks (host, determinism, CPU-set, cross-host, smoke, pilot basis); the deadlines of jobs and campaigns (for example the deadline option of the screening measurement); time limits on probe attempts; latest-start times; and time limits on LLM calls. A job or a call that shows no progress is ended and issued again by its operator as an infrastructure failure (R1-CARD-SHARE (3)); a call issued again does not count against an LLM-call cap. (2) **The run budget of a proposal stays part of the task, and it never decides on a shared card** (option TIMEOUT A of the coordinating chat's sheet of 26 Nov, chosen by the author). The per-run budget of the label campaigns and of the closed loop stays as each table fixed it: for T1, R1-G1B-TIMEOUT's 3 × the slowest census record; for T0, the 600 s of the audit worker, under which the frozen labels of sweeps 1 and 2 were produced. This budget is what makes a proposal that trains many times longer, or never ends, a failed proposal, and it is the budget the frozen labels and the G.4b pilot used. A run that reaches its budget, or runs out of memory, while another compute process was on its card is an infrastructure failure and is repeated. It counts as the proposal's failure (for a baseline run or a G.1b run: as the STOP of R1-G1B-TIMEOUT) only on an attempt that had its card to itself. The reviewed specs keep their values (expansion G.1b at launch commit `7d0279d8`; the G.5 draft, revision 13). (3) **GPU-hour caps do not stop work.** A shared or slower card raises GPU-hours without changing a result. For work not yet under a signed note (expansion G.1b 40, G.5 425, P.4 phase 1 40, D.3a's 140 projection), the figure is a reporting threshold: usage is reported against it, and reaching it is reported to the author at once. Where reviewed code stops at the figure, the coordinator publishes an addendum that raises it, unless it finds a defect; this row pre-authorises that addendum. (4) A guard on a helper call (a git command, a status poll, a health probe) that can only raise an infrastructure failure is not a limit in the sense of this row; reviewed code is not rewritten for it. (5) Amends, for work not yet launched: R1-G1B-TIMEOUT and R1-TRACKG-PORT-DECISIONS (4) as in (2); the caps of R1-T1-NODE-EXPANSION, R1-G5-DECISIONS, R1-TRACKP-P4-DECISIONS and R1-MINSUB-CHOICES as in (3); and every rule that gives a check, a job or a call a time limit, as in (1). |
| R1-STEP-ORDER | **No calendar date decides anything; the work proceeds in the order of its steps** (author, 26 Nov 2026: "把…日期限制都全部抹除，按步骤流程一步一步向下走"). (1) **Withdrawn as rules:** the expansion cutoff of 2 Dec (R1-T1-NODE-EXPANSION, R1-G5-EXPANSION-JOIN, R1-BX-D14-G4B); the arms-frozen cutoff of 20 Dec and the release of 17 Dec (R1-ARMS-FREEZE-CUTOFF); the 3 Jan rule (R1-SUBMISSION-MIN, R1-D3-MIN, R1-CONTROLS-ROW, R1-D3-ORDER) and the dated fallbacks of R1-POSTFREEZE-READS and R1-POSTFREEZE-READS-CHOICES; the fallback date of R1-BX-D10; the dates of R1-INTERNAL-FREEZES, R1-BX-D14 and R1-BX-D14-G4B; and "about 10 Dec" in R1-MINSUB-CHOICES. A step starts when the steps it depends on are complete and reviewed. No step is cut, shortened or reordered to meet a date. (2) **What the dates decided is decided by completion.** (a) *The T1 node set* is the eight nodes and every expansion candidate whose G.1b completes with determinism PASS. Such a candidate joins G.5 through the addendum note whenever its G.1b completes; one that does not pass goes to the funnel as before. (b) *Order to the gate:* the G.5 labels complete for every node of the set; then the T1 train/dev release row; then the arms trained, selected and calibrated; then the arms-frozen record; then the one gate read. No node is left out for being late, so R1-ARMS-FREEZE-CUTOFF (b) to (d) have no case. A node leaves the set only by an author row that rests on label-free facts, and its records then stay sealed as (c) of that row says. The proof checks of R1-MINSUB-CHOICES are recorded before the gate read, as before. (c) *Optional work.* The submission version is complete when parts (1) to (4) of R1-SUBMISSION-MIN are complete and reviewed, part (4) with D.3b (R1-D3B-UNCONDITIONAL). An optional item (D.3c, R1-D3-HPO-REF, the controls, the optional ablations, a further proof check) enters if it is complete and reviewed at that point; it never delays those parts. (d) *Dated fallbacks.* Where a row gave a fallback "if X does not exist by a date" (the full C1 of R1-POSTFREEZE-READS; the Track S result of R1-BX-D10), the fallback applies if X does not exist when the submission version is assembled. (3) **The venue's dates are not rules of this plan.** ICML 2027 (abstract 14 Jan, full paper 22 Jan 2027) stays the target of R1-SUBMISSION-MIN in this sense only: the paper is submitted there if the submission version is complete in time. If it is not, the author chooses the venue then; TMLR (R1-VENUE-2) takes submissions at any time. (4) The date model and the duty to recompute dates (R1-BX-D14 (iii), R1-BX-D14-G4B) end. The digest reports which steps are complete and what each open step waits for. A date that moves is no longer an item for the author (R1-AUTONOMY (2)); a step that cannot proceed is. |

## 11. Relation to Paper A

- Paper A stays an independent measurement paper.
- R1 cites A for replay identification (anonymously while both are under review) and uses it for branch labels and restore audits.
- R1 does not depend on A's live replay panel.
- Paper A's clean-vs-clean nondeterminism floor is related evidence. R1 measures its own noise floors per node.
